# Volume 05 character state — Batch 0001, Chapters 201 to 210

**The state as it stands at the end of the eleventh of the fourth month of the eighteenth year after the Long Fracture. Descriptive labels are the prose's own and are how the chapters name these people; the names in this file are for the writer and are not in the prose.**

## The man the figure of twenty-one years is against

**Not named in the prose. Thirty-four. The figure of twenty-one years came off the fourth line of the fourth sheet in the ward market's own book on the thirty-first of March, in the same line as the date, because a date arrived, and nothing was written in its place, and the district kept the label and the prose kept using it.** He stood at the back of a public book against the tolls of a water route on the twenty-seventh of the third month; his own hand is on a page in a drawer in a building with a door on it, written on the evening of the twenty-eighth; a house's eighth sheet says the tolls are fastened to the person at the back of that book and names him. **He can pool tolls. He cannot create consent. He has one departure left and has not spent it, the refusals to read stand at nine, the departure is unspent, and there is still no date in the ledger for the next reading.** He was named a steward on the nineteenth of October by nine households and not by himself; the second half of the word is a season, the season started on the first of April, and **nobody has stood in a frame since the nineteenth of October and a clerk has entered three times that this is not a delay.**

**In this block he is wrong twice, both on the page, and neither is a stupid error.** He refused a bearer twice in a yard, on the fourth and the sixth of April, **and on the seventh a stranger says out loud that he was never asked for one, in front of nineteen people, and the man the figure of twenty-one years is against says nothing at all for nine seconds, which is the only time in ten chapters that he does not speak when he could.** He is not a party to that exchange; a man of fifty-six entered that he was not asked and is not going to be. On the eighth he asks the registrar to put a name on a line for a trip with no term on it, having been told on the second that a name on a line is a name off a sheet. **She refuses in four seconds in eighty-two words and he says afterwards that he knew the answer before he asked and asked in front of nineteen people anyway, and she says that is a thing clerks do and she did not enjoy it either.** On the tenth he asks her for one thing and she grants it in four seconds. **He is competent, he is not a hero of this block, and his two errors are the block's argument: an instrument used on the wrong question, and a fix that is the disease.**

## The registrar of this district's records office

**Not named in the prose. Her instrument is a room. An office may print a house and may not print a person, and she has said that in this yard four times in five months. She says no in four seconds and gives the reason before she is asked — three times in this block, on the second, the eighth and the tenth of April, and three times in eleven weeks including the fifteenth of the second month. She grants twice in the block, in four seconds each.** She is the only person in this district whose instrument is a room, and he is the only person who has put his own name at the back of one, and those two facts came together on the twenty-eighth of the third month and have not come apart.

**The block's movement, and it is four steps and none of them is a declaration.** On the second she refuses a stranger a page in thirty-seven words. On the seventh, in a lane with no clerk within nine feet and nobody counting, he asks whether she refused him the page or refused the Registry, and she says in thirty-two words that a stranger may look and may not carry, that he is not a stranger, that that is the whole of the difference, and that she is not going to say the rest of it in a lane. On the eighth she refuses a name on a line in eighty-two words. On the tenth she grants the eight printed days into her own book in sixty-one words, **and the grant is the first instrument of this volume that anybody in the yard argued for.** The two of them have been in the same yard at the same hour on purpose nine times since the eighteenth of December and none of the four is one of the nine and none is to be entered as one. **A slate went past a kitchen in Alder Reach eleven times in the month before this one and neither of them wrote on it and it is not to be asked about. What was said between them on the seventh of April is in a minute in her own words if she chooses to write it and is not a document this district can produce if she does not.** Unresolved. **The volume must not resolve it and must not end on a settlement.**

## The man of about thirty-four who digs loam

**Not named. The relationship the last two volumes made and the one this volume leans on: he finds the sentence, he says the plainest thing anybody has said, and he would rather be carrying a yoke.** In this block he is the mechanism of the volume and the man who refuses every join anybody offers him. He refused the ninth place to the ninth holding, the fourth holding to the road, the eight days to the road and the dray to the eight days, and he refused a row that says it is not a road to a holding nobody can account for, and he said the same reason each time: **a resemblance noticed a second time is not a finding and a resemblance noticed a third time is a habit.** He disagrees out loud with the panels at Chapters 205 and 207 and does not disagree with the one at Chapter 210, and a clerk entered the fact. **He was asked nothing in any of the ten chapters and refused no ask, because there is no ask to refuse.** He said the plainest sentence in the block in Chapter 208 — a piece of wood on a cart that came out of a yard is a job, and the job is hers because it came off her stones — and the other one in Chapter 210, to a lane at six in the morning: **I would like somebody to write down that I said I would not and said it anyway, in my own words. Nobody wrote it down.** He knows four generations of people keep the things that hold the water back and that none of them has ever been on a page. **He carries no instrument, is in no count, and would not have it written down.**

## The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing

**Not named. He asks the six things and he gives the figures out loud, and he is the man who found in Chapter 202 that this district's best instrument is a door.** In this block he said in this yard on the eighth that he would ask the woman of fifty-eight himself in the morning and in his own words and not in the six things, **and on the ninth he did not, and a clerk entered that he did not and that nobody asked him why and that a man who says a thing in a yard at four in the afternoon has not made a promise.** On the ninth he took two empty buckets off her and carried them up the hill and was not asked to, and counted from the bottom of the bank that she had been up it eleven times between about nine and about one, and was not asked to say he had counted. **He asked a man of about thirty at the counter for a word and got a word, and did not ask him for a man, and a man of fifty-six entered that the two of those are different and the difference is the whole of the difference and nobody in that yard has said it out loud before.** He is one of the four people the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing's own yard counts as doing the work for nothing. **He is in no list and refused entry into all three on the page.**

## The man of fifty-six

**Not named. Secretary of a cooperative of nine households. Not an officer of anything, not a friend of anybody in that yard, and not a clerk, and he has said so about himself eleven times in four months.** He gives the day-counts out loud, he reads a stranger's sheet out in the ordinary voice without being asked, and he is the man who says the second half of a thing. In this block he also said in seventy-two words that **a no in a yard is a thing that happened and is not a thing that holds**, and in Chapter 207 that **a man who has been refused the wrong thing for five days is not a man who has been refused.** **At the end of the block a man of about thirty-four who digs loam asked him to write down the last true sentence anybody said about a dray going up a road, and he could not, and nobody else wrote it.** **He is the only character in this block whose one request is refused, and it is refused by the absence of a clerk and not by a decision.**

## The man of about thirty-one who mends a dray at a forge end off Salt Row

**Not named. The fifth of the five posted securities and the fifth of the five things this district does not have. He has been a security since the ninth year on a bond whose third line says the person giving it shall not be asked, and he was never told, and a house thirty-four miles east holds a receipt saying he was discharged with the thing in the fourteenth year while this district's own page says the ninth year, and the two figures are five years and nine years and are not joined.** He went out at first light on the first of April with a stub about the size of a coin and **walked two days of road to a counter and had his own name taken off a sheet in about nine seconds at no cost, and came home on the third with a strip of paper about the length of his hand that says the name came off, and said in fifty-one words that he has not been released and has been found.** A name put on a figure cannot be taken off it by the person it is put on, and what came off here is not the figure and is the name. He said it can be taken off where they put it on and not by him, and that he is not glad. He said on the fifth, in forty-four words, that he does not know what a person at the back of a book is for.

**In this block he became the district's first accidental bearer of a job that was not his.** He offered a dray on the seventh and asked for it to be written down that nobody has to pay him. **He took a yoke off a woman's stones at about eleven on the ninth without asking her, said so out loud in fifty-four words, and asked for it in his own words and not the clerk's.** He put it on a tailboard in the open on the tenth because he had said he would not put it on a cart until she had seen it on a cart, and she did, and he said in fifty words that he has not got a way to pay her for the two days and is not going to pretend he has. **He drove the dray up the only road that climbs at about half past five on the eleventh of April with the stranger beside him.** He is a man and not a bearer and is in no count of any kind. **He is the man the Volume 05 midpoint reversal is reserved for and it is not spent: he has not been in a room with the registrar since the fourth of April, and a Registry put a name on a page and the name was his and he read it in a room with the registrar in it on a day in January.**

## The woman of fifty-eight

**Not named. Carried four yoke-loads a day up a hill for nine years. Her round of about ninety houses ended on the twenty-fourth of the third month because a water came down a channel; this district arranged to take it off her, did not ask her, and has nothing to give her in place of it. A man of fifty-six said on the twenty-first of the second month that the district had not solved anything and had moved the bill, and that this has not stopped being true.** A woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale offered her four houses of the round on the thirty-first of the third month, has not repeated it, and says four houses out of about ninety is not a round and is not going to become a round.

**She has been put five things in a yard across the volume and this block gives her three of them, and she has said the same shape of true sentence three times in eleven days to three different men, and a man of about thirty-four who digs loam says on the page that a woman who says the same true thing three times is not a woman who has run out of things to say and is a woman who has run out of people who can price one day.** On the first of April she said in sixty-four words that she cannot say what a day of it is worth and is not going to take a thing she cannot say the worth of. On the ninth she said in sixty-five words that the yoke is hers and that she is not saying no to a man in a yard and is not saying yes and is telling the whole of what she knows and the whole of it is that. On the tenth she stood at the bottom of a lane for about a minute and a quarter and said nothing at all, and when she was asked, in the six things, whether she would come up the hill and walk the yoke down herself, she said no in four seconds in thirty-two words: **then it is not mine to walk down, I have said for eleven days that I cannot say what a day of it is worth and this is a day of it.** She walked up a bank eleven times with one bucket in each hand. **Her round is not coming back. The offer of four houses still stands and has not been taken up. Her yoke is on a dray that went up a road on the eleventh and nobody in this block says when it comes back. She is not a protected thing and is not one of the five things this district does not have and is not one of the four conditions with no end on it, and was refused entry into all four lists in the prose.**

## The man with the portfolio

**Not named. About forty-four, a lease clerk of a Registry, thin, carrying a flat case the size of a hymn book.** Nine days in the district, from the second to the eleventh of April, **sleeping five nights on a bench outside the door with the fanlight over it that nobody gave him.** He was never asked to leave and never asked to stay and the bench is not a room and is not entered. He was put in the six things on the second, the fourth, the fifth, the sixth and the seventh, on five different days, and the panel's rule that a person may be asked once in the same six things was entered each time and the count of the refusals to read was nine every time. **He never answered the third of the six things, which is the year, and gave no reason. He answered the fourth, the term. He did not answer the fifth, the toll. He answered the sixth, who he came for.** He refused the place three times, on the second, the third and the seventh, and never once refused outright. **He said on the seventh that in five days in this district nobody once asked him what he came for, and that what he came for was not a bearer, and that what he came for was a page, and that the yard spent five days refusing a man instead of a page.** He said in thirty-four words that his body enters a no and dates it and that the asking is the answer. He said in fifty-six words that a keeper is a person who is on a hill and is not on a page and that there are four generations of them where his lease is and none of them has ever been on a page of his. **He does not know if the holder can be reached and did not say. He said he had a name and did not say where he got it, and that that is not a refusal, it is a door on his side. He left at about half past five on the eleventh of April on a dray up a road he will not name, and nothing in this block says where the road goes or when he comes back.** He is a competitor, not an antagonist, and he was competent and honest and cost this district a page, a name, a word it had been using and a morning.

## The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale

**Not named. Has weighed nine sacks a day for nine years at the sluice end of the loams and has never weighed a day. She is the only person in this district who has ever been corrected by her own question in a yard, and she said so out loud in Chapter 205: she had said OPEN four times and asked herself what was opening.** She offered the woman of fifty-eight four houses on the thirty-first of the third month, said she had said it once and was not going to say it twice, and said that four houses out of about ninety is not a round and is not going to become a round and that there is nothing the matter with the work and the whole of the matter is the figure and that she can weigh nine sacks a day and cannot weigh a day. In Chapter 208 she said in fifty-four words that the yoke in this district is a carrying yoke that has never pulled anything and that a dray wants a draught yoke and this district has not got one. **She is the person who got the word OPEN out of a stranger's schedule, and the reason this district has the keepers' word instead of the Registry's is that a woman at a weighbridge heard a heading and asked what it meant. She is in no count and is not a bearer and is not one of the five things this district does not have and was refused entry on the page.**

## The man of about thirty at the counter

**Not named. Carries four sheets for a house thirty-four miles east down the eleven mile road, on the second, the fourth, the sixth and the ninth of April in this block. He was not sent for any of them and this district has no schedule for him and never had one.** He refused the ninth holding six times with no reason, the sixth on the fourth of April, and six are six and are not a column and are not added to the thirteen. **That six is the figure for the ninth holding and is not the pool: the refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given stand at seven, the pool having reached six in Volume 04 on the twenty-fourth of the third month and his sixth being the seventh.** **He is at the twelfth time down the road on the fourth of April and the thirteenth on the sixth, forward from a tenth on the twenty-seventh of the third month, and he has been coming since the second of January.** He said on the sixth, in four seconds, that his house had never heard of a Registry, and a clerk entered that the registrar said no in four seconds on the fifteenth of the second month and that a clerk is not going to put a number on the two. **On the ninth he gave this district the word locks, in fifty-three words, and said his house has called them that since before anybody living was born and has a word for the man who keeps one and a word for the night he turns it.** He is not a clerk of a house in the sense the six refusals count, is not the holder, is not the bearer, is not on a page, and was not asked a seventh thing on the ninth and the not-asking is entered.

## The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing

**Not named. Owed eleven coppers a week, refused four times for the same reason, and the fourth refusal for the same reason is not a fourth refusal. He has found four days out in the district's own arithmetic and they were his own.** On the sixth of April he copied the schedule of nine rows in about fifty minutes onto the back of a sheet of his own buying, in his own hand, column heading and eight days and the ninth row with its two words, gave it to a clerk of nineteen years, and said twenty-one words: **I gave it up, nobody took it, put both of those down and put down which one of them is true.** **That copy is the thing the eight days were read out of on the ninth of April, and the eight days went into a book on the tenth.** On the tenth he said he copied eight days and does not know what a lock is and would have copied it just the same and that this is not a thing anybody is owed money for and that he has said so three times and is not going to say it a fourth. **He is the cheapest thing this district has ever got and the district is not going to pay him, and both of those are entered and neither is a finding.**

## The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households

**Not named. At the sluice end of the loams. Has been asked three times to be told what to do about three households and has not been told, and the asking is the house's and not this district's, and this yard decided on the nineteenth of the second month not to decide whether the three are going to be asked again and has not gone back on it. Not deciding is a decision and was entered as one at the time.** The six stand at one signed, two refused, three not asked again, unchanged since the twenty-first of the third month, and one of the three is a man who lives in a cart. In this block he was in the yard on the sixth about two in the afternoon, was not sent for and was not asked anything. **A man of not asking is not a man who has been refused and is not a count of anything and the record says not asked.**

## The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat at the third house up the lane from the scale

**Not named. One of the two that refused a sheet in the second month. Said on the third of April, in eighteen words, that she would rather be in the water with her name not written on anything than pay for it. She is in a pool of tolls with her name not written on anything and nobody has asked her about the pool and the not-asking is entered.** On the eleventh of April she stood at her own door in the dark at about half past five in the morning and watched a dray go up a road with a yoke on the back of it, and was not sent for and was not asked anything. **She is not a protected thing and is not in any of the three lists and was refused entry on the page, and she said on the second of April that being here is not the same as agreeing to it and that nobody has asked her which one she is doing.**

## The route keeper

**Not named in Volume 05's prose. A keeper of a road and not a bearer of it. He appears in person in Chapter 201 on the fourth walk in five weeks and nine miles, and says in forty-five words that somebody opened his road on the night of the twenty-second of the second month and that it was not him and that he has not seen who it was and that he is not going to say a name.** He was asked one question and gave one answer. A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said that a road opening is not a thing and a road being open is a thing, and that a person who opens a road and is not asked who opened it is not the same as a person who is not asked. **He is not in the yard again in this block. In Chapter 205 a man of fifty-six said he is nine miles off and would be the man to ask and nobody sent for him, and a clerk entered that the word was not put to him and that he was not asked and that the record says not asked.** **He is the man the Volume 05 outline reserves for two batches and the man who is right about a dangerous route later in the volume; he is not a bearer of the road and is not going to be asked to be one, and Batch 0002 owes him eight of nine dates differently and a ninth he will not give.**

## The man of about fifty-five who keeps a building four miles down a lane

**Not named. Refused a fifth mend in his tank three times, the third time on the first of April with the crack dry for the first time since the twenty-eighth of the second month, and gave no reason.** He said in forty-six words on the fifth of April that he has watched that tank for nine years and has never once been able to say where the water came from, and that a channel is a channel and the road beside it is a road and the two of them meet about a mile up. He then said that a tank with a mark on it is a tank he can see and a tank with a mend in it is a tank that comes out in three pieces, and that he has nine years of beds in this building and has not lost one. **A building that has denied nobody entry in nine years and has not lost a bed in nine years is a protected thing in the ordinary sense and is not a protected thing in the count, and the protected count is five and no sixth was added.**

## A man of about nineteen who counts

**Not named. He is the reason every figure in this block is checkable. In this block he counted forty-four sentences and all forty-four reproduce. He is nobody's keeper and is not to be turned into one.**

## Off the page in this block, and to be left there

**Cael Orin, the leaseholder, whose name was said once out loud in a yard in Chapter 203 and not written down; a name said once in a yard is a name four hundred people have, and a name said twice is a name a counter can ask for, and this district has had the good kind since the twentieth of December without knowing it.** Iven, presumed dead from Adrian's point of view throughout, is not named, not referred to and not foreshadowed in any of the ten chapters. The office is in none of them. Lina and Orren are in none of them. **Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block and nothing required him.** **The protected category stays closed at five and no sixth was added and four refusals of a sixth are on the page, in Chapters 205, 208, 209 and 210.**
