# Volume 05 Batch 0002 — character state, Chapters 211 to 220

**Every entry below is additive to the Batch 0001 section above it and supersedes it only where it names a figure. No person is named in this block's prose and no person is named here.**

## The man the figure of twenty-one years is against

**Refused in seventy-eight words, in the six things, in front of about nineteen people, to have this district's answer to a council decided by a yard: *I am not going to say no in a yard about a thing nobody has asked me properly, and a body that wants a man out of this district has to come to this district and put the question to me in a room with a clerk in it, and I am not going to be a man who is sent to a council because about nineteen people stood in a yard and somebody read out a letter.*** A refusal with a reason. A clerk of nineteen years entered that the count of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given did not move because of it, and that a man who says he will not be sent to a council by a yard has not said he will not go to a council and cannot be counted either way. He read the council's letter out in a yard on the sixteenth of April because a yard reads a document when somebody asks it to. He gave her a different page in a lane on the eighteenth. He has not been in a room with the registrar since the fourth of April and does not come near one in this block. He has one departure left and has not spent it, the refusals to read stand at nine, and there is still no date in the ledger for the next reading.

## The registrar of this district's records office

**ONE MOVEMENT IN THIS BLOCK AND IT IS NOT A DECLARATION.** In a lane behind the forge end off Salt Row on the eighteenth of April, with no clerk within nine feet of either of them and nobody counting, **she asked him for the page of the ward market's own book where the figure of twenty-one years came off on the thirty-first of last month, in his own hand, to put in her own book, and he gave her the other side of the same leaf**, which carries the date and his own hand and nothing written under it, and said that it is a page and it is not the one she asked for. A clerk of nineteen years entered afterwards that two people said two things in a lane and that nobody counted them. A man of fifty-six entered that a man who gives a woman a different page from the one she asked for has told her what a page says and has not told her what she asked about, and that those are two different things. **She refused nothing and granted nothing in these ten chapters. She has refused in four seconds three times in eight weeks and four days — the fifteenth of the second month, the second of April and the eighth — and there was no fourth refusal and no grant.** **She refused, in seventy-three words, to have the eleven housekeepers' names said in a yard, on this district's own rule about a name said twice.** She was in the yard on six days running in Chapter 211 and was not asked a first thing on any of them and said nothing in it. She said one thing in a yard on the twenty-first, unasked, in fifty-one words: that her book now holds eight printed days off a stranger's sheet and nine printed nights off a body's own paper, and that a person may look at both in that office and may not carry either out of it. **The romance is unresolved and the volume must not end on a settlement.**

## The man of about thirty-four who digs loam

**Said the sum out loud on the twelfth of April, in seventy-six words, and said a run that goes one a night does not stop at eight because it is the eighth, and said that he said it in a lane on the eleventh and nobody wrote it down and it is still not written down.** He refused to put the road house's book next to the stranger's sheet and gave the reason: **two records that are not about the same thing cannot agree and cannot disagree.** He put the standing orders next to the stranger's schedule in a yard in Chapter 213 and said out loud that two lists of nine that agree on eight are not one list and the one they disagree about is the ninth, and that a man who has stood at the back of a public book for four months has never once put two pieces of paper next to each other and read out what the two of them said. He refused the fourth-holding join a third time in twelve days and said the third time is a habit. He refused the board in fifty-five words: a date is not a finding. He announced his own resemblance about the tank twice before anybody could find it, in a hundred and four words, and said **I am not going to let a pail of water be the witness.** He said on the nineteenth that he has disagreed with one panel in fourteen days and has not disagreed with one, and that the one he has not disagreed with is the one about a person, and that he was not going to say any more about that today than he had just said. **He asked three times in ten days for a sentence of his own to be written down and it was not written down three times, the last time because a man of fifty-six is not a clerk.** He is a man and not a bearer and is in none of the seven lists.

## The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing

**KEPT THE PROMISE BATCH 0001 LEFT OPEN.** On the eighth of April he said in a yard that he would ask the woman of fifty-eight himself in the morning and in his own words, and on the ninth he did not and a clerk entered that he did not and that nobody asked him why. **On the twelfth he named a day out loud in a yard — the sixteenth — and said so that a clerk could enter that a day was said. On the sixteenth he walked up the bank at about half past eight with nothing in his hands and asked her, in his own words and not in the six things, how many of the ninety houses she can still name, in sixty words, and said he had not asked her what she wants and was not going to ask her a fourth time about a thing he cannot price.** A man of fifty-six entered that the two of those are the whole difference between a man and a yard. **He said in ninety-six words that no keeper has ever been on a page of anybody's and that it is not because pages forget people.** He said in fifty-four words that a body that writes to a village is a body that knows the village has something. He counted eleven trips up the bank on the ninth of April and was not asked to say he had counted. He did not carry the buckets for her on the nineteenth and a man of fifty-six entered that a man who has stopped doing a thing he was never asked to do has not refused anything and the record says not asked. He went up the only road that climbs on the nineteenth at about five in the afternoon and came down it in the dark without sleeping.

## The man of fifty-six

**Secretary of a cooperative of nine households, not an officer of anything, not a friend of anybody in that yard, and not a clerk, and he has said so about himself eleven times in four months.** He gives the figures out loud and reads the board out afterwards and gets both of them right in every chapter of this block. He said in Chapter 216 that a yard that about nineteen people can stand in and decide something about a woman who is not in it is a door, and that the door which got used last month was not the one anybody was proud of. He said on the sixteenth of April that a letter is a document this district has got and did not write and that a document this district did not write is a document this district cannot produce, and that those are two different sentences and neither is a fourth document nobody owns. He said that two records that are not about the same thing cannot agree and cannot disagree. He was asked three times in this block to write a sentence down and could not, and the not-writing is entered three times. He read the board out four times in Chapter 213 and did the sum in no chapter and was not asked to.

## The man of about thirty-one who mends a dray at a forge end off Salt Row

**Came down the only road in this district that climbs at about half past three in the afternoon on the fourteenth of April with one man on the box, the yoke lashed to the tailboard, and a folded printed sheet under the yoke that had not been on the cart when it went up. The man with the portfolio was not on it and the case was not on it.** He said in fifty-two words that he was given a sheet, that he did not ask for it, that he has not read it, that he is not going to say who gave it to him, and that the man who went up with him is not on the cart and he did not ask him to come down either. **He put the strip of paper on the trestle table face up on the fourteenth, unasked, and nobody asked him about it.** He refused in fifty-four words to say whether the house in the keeper's column is the same house nine times or nine houses, and in seventy words to say where the road goes, and in fifty words to work out what a column of houses means from the back of a cart in a yard. **All three refusals carried a reason and none of them is in the count of eight.** He said he was going up at about half past five on the twentieth on his own account and was not taking anybody, and said *I am not going to be asked about it this morning* before anybody had asked him. **He went up at about half past five on the twentieth with a yoke left in the forge and was not back on the twenty-first.** He is a man and not a bearer and is not one of the five things this district does not have and was refused entry into all the lists on the page.

## The woman of fifty-eight

**On the sixteenth of April said, in forty-one words, that eleven of about ninety houses still have a door on them and that she could go back to those eleven tomorrow and is not going to be asked for the other seventy-nine. Asked whether he was to write the eleven down, she refused in four seconds in seventy-three words: a name said once in a yard is a name four hundred people have and a name said twice is a name a counter can ask for, and you taught that to this yard on the third of this month, and I am not saying eleven names in front of nineteen people so that a thing I have kept in my head since the second month is in a book in a room.** On the seventeenth **she took eleven houses of her round in four seconds, in sixty words, without pricing them, and disagreed with a panel out loud in fifty-one words — the third person in the volume to disagree with one — and said I am not a round and I never said I was.** She split the eleven into six above the channel and five below it. On the nineteenth she said in twenty-four words that she will do eleven, one at a time, eleven times, and that she has been doing one at a time eleven times since the ninth anyway. On the twenty-first she said in thirty-three words that she started at seven in the morning, will do eleven, and that this is the first morning anybody has left it alone. **Nobody has priced a day of any of it. The fifth of the five things this district does not have is a way to pay a person who is not in a household and it did not move. The round of about seventy-nine is not coming back and about ninety is not a figure, so that is not a subtraction. The offer of four houses from the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale still stands, has not been taken up, and four and eleven were refused entry as an addition on the page.** She is not a protected thing and was refused entry in four lists on the page. She is not a keeper and is not named on the keepers' own page, and that is the block's finding.

## The man with the portfolio

**Not back.** He went up the road on the eleventh of April with a yoke on the tailboard and did not come down it on the fourteenth, and nothing in this block says where he is or when he comes back. **The fourteenth of April would have been his thirteenth day, counting from the day he came in, and a clerk of nineteen years entered that a day a man is not here is not a day he has been here and did not count the thirteenth for him.** He slept five nights on a bench outside the door with the fanlight over it and six more on a dray, and neither figure is a figure about a person. He is off the page from Chapter 214 onward. **He is a competitor and not an antagonist, and this block did not use him and did not cheapen his return.**

## The man of about thirty at the counter

**The fifteenth time down the eleven mile road, on the fifteenth of April, a hundred and three days after the second of January, which is fourteen weeks and five days. The whole run is named and dated on the page: eleventh on the second, twelfth on the fourth, thirteenth on the sixth, fourteenth on the ninth, fifteenth today — and a clerk of nineteen years entered that this district has been carrying his visits in a state file for four months and has never once counted them off a day.** He refused the ninth holding for the seventh time with no reason, **and the count of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given moved from seven to eight for the first time since the twenty-fourth of last month.** A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing gave the reason out loud in sixty-four words: the man has been refused seven times in four months and has never once been asked why, and the reason he gives no reason is that nobody in this district has ever asked him for one. He said in thirty-eight words that a house is what keeps a lock when a man cannot, and that his house has never said a keeper's name on anything and he has asked about it twice in thirty-four miles and nobody would tell him a name. He did not come on the twenty-first and the record says not asked. **He is not a clerk of a house in the sense the refusals count in any other body, is not the holder, is not the bearer, is not on a page, and is not on the keepers' page either.**

## The route keeper

**Walked nine miles on the thirteenth of April, the fifth time he has walked it, and gave this district the eight nights he has walked the road in this run, and refused the ninth in forty-two words with a reason: *That one is a night another man wrote and not a night I walked, and I keep a road and I am not a keeper of a book, and I am not taking a man's page round a yard like this one.*** He gave two more words unasked, in fifty: his house has a word for the man who turns one and a word for the night he turns it, and neither of those two words is on any page. **These are the same two words the man of about thirty at a counter gave this district on the ninth of April, out of a different house, and the review pass made that visible on the page: the fencing man's line in Chapter 212 used to say the counter man did not give the other two, which was false, and it now says he gave the word and two more with it and that a man nine miles off gave the same two again out of a different house. Two houses saying the same two words is the kind of thing this district exists to notice, and Chapter 220's man of fifty-six now gives the pair to the counter man and the not-on-any-page half to this man.** He said in forty-six words that he walks the road when there is water in the channel and that on the twelfth and the thirteenth there was none, and that this is a reason and is not a keeper's reason and he would know. He said in thirty-six words that he was nine miles off the road on the eleventh and that there was no wheel track on it that had not been there when he came down. **He is not a bearer of a road and has never been asked to be one, and a refusal of his with a reason is not one of the eight.**

## The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale

**Asked what four houses of a round is worth a day for the third time in thirteen days and gave the same answer for the third time, in forty-three words: I have weighed nine sacks a day for nine years and I have never weighed a day, and four is not eleven and eleven is not ninety and nobody in this yard is going to make me weigh one of them this afternoon.** She said in fifty-two words on the eighteenth that she can weigh the water in both buckets and cannot weigh the walk up the bank eleven times, and that a man who sells the wrong one on purpose is doing the district a kindness. **Her four houses still stand, have not been taken up, and were not folded into eleven on the page.** She is the only person in this district who has ever been corrected by her own question and is not a bearer and is not a seventh instrument and was refused entry on the page.

## The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households

**Said in fifty-two words that the house has to ask him, that he has been asked four times now to be told what to do about the three and has not been told, and that he would like it written down that he has not been told four times and that a fourth time is not a fifth.** **The six stand at one signed, two refused, three not asked again, unchanged since the twenty-first of last month, which is twenty-two days.** A man of not asking is not a man who has been refused and is not a count of anything and the record says not asked.

## The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing

**On the twelfth, asked what a lock is, said in sixty-three words that he does not know, that he has copied the word nine times and knows it is spelt with a K and that the column it is over says OPEN, and that he would rather have it written down that he has got it than have somebody decide he has not. On the twenty-first he copied the standing orders in about forty minutes onto the back of a sheet of his own buying, in his own hand, column heading and the column headed KEEPER and all nine nights, gave the copy to a clerk of nineteen years, and said twelve words: *Nobody took it. I gave it away. Put both of those down.*** A clerk read the copy against the sheet and got nothing wrong. **This district now holds two copies of two different documents in two different rooms four hundred yards apart and the three documents nobody owns did not move.** **Owed eleven coppers a week, refused four times for the same reason, and the fourth refusal for the same reason is not a fourth refusal. He has said he will not say it a fifth time.**

## The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat

**Was in the yard on the eighteenth of April, was not sent for, was not asked anything, and said in sixty-one words that she stood in the cold because she was awake and that the clerk is not to make it into anything in a yard.** She is in a pool of tolls with her name not written on anything and has been since the thirtieth of last month, and nobody has asked her about the pool.

## The woman of about thirty-eight who keeps a building four miles down a lane

**Said on the twelfth of April, in thirty-nine words, that the butt at the back of that building is for the water that comes off the roof, that it is four days and it was nine, and that she has not asked anybody for a fifth day and is not going to.** Not asked a first thing about her wall since the first of April.

## The man of about fifty-five who keeps that building

**The tank came up within a hand's breadth of its lip on the twentieth of April for the second time since the first of the second month and the crack is running. Asked whether he wanted a fifth mend in it now, he said no in about two seconds and gave a reason in fifty words: a tank with a mark on it is a tank I can see and a tank with a mend in it is a tank that comes out in three pieces, and I have said that in January and on the first of this month and I am saying it now. He then said, unasked, that he has nine years of beds in that building and has not lost one, and that the water is where the water comes from and he has never once been able to say where that is and he is fifty-five and has stopped expecting to.** A clerk of nineteen years entered that she is not going to count how many times he has said no to a fifth mend. **A refusal with a reason, and a man who keeps a building is not a clerk of a house and a building that has denied nobody entry in nine years is a building and not a sixth protected thing.**

## A man of about nineteen who counts

**The reason every figure in this block is checkable. In this block he counted forty-five sentences and all forty-five reproduce. He is nobody's keeper and is not to be turned into one.**

## Off the page in this block, and to be left there

**Cael Orin, the leaseholder, whose name was said once out loud in a yard in Chapter 203 and was NOT said a second time in Chapters 211 to 220, and the yard's own rule about a name said twice was used in Chapter 215 by a woman of fifty-eight refusing to say eleven housekeepers' names.** Iven, presumed dead from Adrian's point of view throughout, is not named, not referred to and not foreshadowed in any of the ten chapters. The office is in none of them. Lina and Orren are in none of them. **Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block and nothing required him.** The record caves, the founder's chair, the nine design signatures, the tolls arriving in villages that did not sign, the nine local charters and the regional covenant are in none of the ten chapters. **The protected category stays closed at five and no sixth was added, and the block refused a sixth four times on the page, in Chapters 214, 216, 219 and 220.**

---

# Volume 05 Batch 0003 — character state, Chapters 221 to 230

**The prose names nobody. Every person below is referred to in Chapters 221 to 230 exactly as in Chapters 201 to 220: the man the figure of twenty-one years is against, the registrar of this district's records office, the man who keeps the road, the man of about thirty-one who mends a dray, the man of about thirty-four who digs loam, the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing, the man of fifty-six, the clerk of nineteen years, the woman of fifty-eight, the man with the portfolio, the man of about thirty at the counter, the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale, the man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households, the woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat, the woman of about thirty-eight who keeps a building, the man of about fifty-five who keeps that building, the man of about twenty-three who copies for nothing, and a man of about nineteen who counts. A boy of about nine carried four words in this block and is not a bearer and is not a clerk and is not the boy whose name is in a column two days of road away.**

## The man the figure of twenty-one years is against

**Refused the chair twice and the two refusals are on the page and are not the same refusal.** **In a room with a shut door in it, in the records office, on the twenty-fourth of April, in a hundred and seventy words:** *I will not put a name in that place and I will not sit in that chair, and I am going to give you the reason once and I am not going to give it to a yard afterwards. A single holder cannot be asked a question by anybody, and a charter with no end on it that begins tomorrow decides what is sown in the season after this one, and the four districts under that water would not be asked about either, and there is nobody in this room to ask. My name is the only name this district has at the back of a book, and a name at the back of a book is a person a toll can be fastened to, and I have been at the back of that book since the twenty-seventh of last month and I know what it feels like from the wrong side, and I am not going to be the reason it feels like that to somebody above the water.* **In the yard on the twenty-eighth, in the six things, in front of about nineteen people, in a hundred and sixty-two words:** *No. I said in this yard on the nineteenth of this month that I would not say no in a yard about a thing nobody had asked me properly, and a body that wants a man out of this district has to come and put it to me in a room. It came to a room on the twenty-fourth and I said no in it, and I am saying no again here, and the two are not the same no. The one in the room was that nobody could ask a single holder a question. This one is that a yard is not a body and nineteen people standing in the cold are not a council, and if I took that chair off that table in front of all of you it would be because you were here and not because a man said no to a piece of paper, and I will not be a founder because a yard is full.* **He said the whole of the room reason again in the yard on the twenty-fourth afternoon, on purpose, so that one of the two repetitions would be in a book.** He asked for a clerk in the room because he had asked for one in a yard on the nineteenth. He refused a second question in four seconds on the twenty-eighth and gave no reason, and a clerk entered that he is not a clerk of a house and that the eight did not move. **He wrote the answer to the Council himself, in four sentences, with a pen that had to be warmed in his hand for a quarter of an hour and ink that went thin twice, and signed it at the bottom with his own name, and nobody asked him to and nobody stopped him.** **The romance did not move in this block and that is deliberate: three rooms with a shut door in them and a man going through the door twice, and nothing said in them that a person could be found for.**

## The registrar of this district's records office

**No movement in the romance in this block, and no refusal and no grant.** She put the offer to him herself, in her own office, in the ordinary voice, and did not read it out of the sheet, and he stood and she did not ask him to sit down. **She said that the clerk is nineteen years old and is at the ward market and has not left it in four months, and that a person in a room is not a clerk and cannot enter anything, and that this is the reason a room is the right place to say a thing like this and is not the wrong one, and that the same rule keeps a stranger out of a book and it is a good rule and it is hers.** She said one thing in that room, uncounted, that he did not answer: *Then somebody in this district is going to be a figure on a page by the last day of this month, because a letter wants a man and a lease wants a man, and I am the only one who can put a name down, and I am not going to put one down that nobody has said to me.* **She refused in eighty words to carry a Registry's page out of her own office, on the ground that the rule is hers, that she wrote it on the twenty-first, and that she wrote it before she knew there was anything on it. She then entered four lines in her own book that do not carry the name and said that is the whole of what that room can do and it is not nothing.** She said in a hundred and eight words that a ledger goes from that room to a counter and back on the four days a month a man of about nineteen is not well, that it has not gone since the twenty-second, and that she is not going to have it carried by a man who has not been asked. She read the lease's non-answer out in ninety-two words. She said in a hundred and two words that the cave page is older than any book in her office and that this district's answer to it is a letter with four words in it and a book with four names and a figure under them, and that neither of them is a copy and neither of them is nine. She is the reason he has not had to be a private man about any of this.

## The man of about thirty-four who digs loam

**His body cost him something and the block is built on it.** He put a spade into a wet bank at the sluice end of the loams at about half past six on the twenty-second and **could not lift a spade above his head for the rest of the block**, and he announced it before anybody had thought to ask him, in sixty-four words. **He carried a lamp up a four-mile climb in his right hand on the twenty-ninth, changed it to his left after about nine minutes, changed it back, and at the bottom of the climb could not get his fingers to close on it for about a minute**, and the man of about thirty-one took it off him without being asked. He tried to lift the gate post with his right arm on the twenty-third and could not, and did not try a second time in front of anybody. He went up a bank at about half past four on the twenty-fifth to carry a bucket he could not carry. He asked a woman of fifty-eight a question on the twenty-ninth carrying a bucket in a sling and she said nothing. He said the room reason was about checkability and the yard reason was about who decides, in sixty-eight words: *a room with a shut door is the only place this district has found where a man can be told no to a thing and mean it, and a yard is the only place this district has found where nineteen people can hear him say it, and he has now been in both, and that is two instruments in a week and neither of them has a name.* He said the offer in eighty-nine words, the third column in a hundred and thirty-eight, the work in eighty-five, the join in eighty-one and ninety-one, and the page in a hundred and six. **He disagreed with a panel out loud for the second time in the volume, on a panel that said a disclosure prevents a thing being done without a person knowing, and a clerk entered that a man who disagrees with a rule twice is not becoming a habit of disagreeing, he is two rules and two disagreements.** **He asked four times in eleven days for a sentence of his own to be written down, on the eleventh, the nineteenth, the twenty-first and the twenty-second, and was not written down four times, and on the twenty-second said out loud that it was not going to happen so that the not-happening would be a thing the yard did. On the thirtieth, without asking, a clerk of nineteen years wrote down what he said about what should go on the sheet in his own words, and he said in forty-four words: That is not the same thing and I know it is not the same thing, and it is the best that has happened to me in a month, and I am not going to say thank you for it in front of nine people.** A man of fifty-six said a man who says he is not going to be thanked in a yard has said that yard's own rule back to it, and that the rule was taught here on the third of this month by a woman of fifty-eight, and that it has now been said by three people in three weeks, and that three is not a column. **A clerk of nineteen years entered on the second of the fifth month that he has said the third-time sentence five times in the volume and that a fifth is a habit of a habit and he is not going to say a sixth.** He got a spade above his head in two goes at about eleven in the morning on the second of the fifth month and nobody counted that either. He is a man and not a bearer and is in none of the seven lists.

## The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing

**Kept the two things he said he would do.** He named the twenty-second out loud in a yard and said a thing would be written down in his own words and it was not. He put the chair question himself in the six things and got the second refusal. He read the Council's second sheet out three times in three days and read the answer out before it went and read the board out on the thirtieth without being asked. He asked a woman of fifty-eight a question in his own words at the foot of a bank and stopped about nine feet short of her, and did not follow her out of the yard. **He sat on a step in the cold from about half past five in the afternoon until the door opened at about nine on the night of the twenty-fifth, with nothing in his hands and no supper, and afterwards said he could see about nine feet of lane and a bucket that was not his.** He repeated a man's disclosure in a yard because the man had not asked him not to, and said out loud that he was not going to ask a man who had just been asked a question a second thing on the same afternoon. He said the block's finding about a room and a yard in sixty-eight words, and the finding about the cave page in a hundred and ten and eighty-two, and he said the ruled ninth place on the cave page is the same shape of nothing as the ninth place on the board, twice, before anybody else did. He carried a chair into a yard and a slate up a road and a lamp's other end when the man who carried it could not hold it.

## The man of fifty-six

**Secretary of a cooperative of nine households, not an officer of anything, not a friend of anybody in that yard, and not a clerk, and he has said so about himself eleven times in four months.** He gives the figures out loud and reads the board and gets it right, and in this block he does it in ten different shapes of sentence because the frame was measured and found. **He said in Chapter 227 that a yard which never sends anybody to a room only ever gets the answers it deserves and that a yard that does send somebody to a room gets a different man back in the afternoon.** He said that a rule a person makes for herself and then uses against somebody else's harm is the only instrument this district has built and not named in four months. He said that a man counting his own unpaid labour is a man keeping a tally and not a clerk and that a clerk cannot count another man's days for him. He said that a man who announces a thing about his own body before anybody can ask him about it is doing the one thing that yard has which is worth more than the yard itself. He said a man who signs his own answer is the only one of that yard who is not a clerk and not a man of about thirty-four and not a woman of fifty-eight. He is in nine of the ten chapters.

## The man of about thirty-one who mends a dray at a forge end off Salt Row

**This is the block and the reversal is his.** He came down the only road in this district that climbs at about half past four in the afternoon on the twenty-fifth, **five days after he went up at about half past five on the morning of the twentieth**, with one man on the box and no yoke on the tailboard, the yoke being in a forge at the end off Salt Row. He stood at the bottom of the lane and said nothing for about a minute. He put the strip of paper back on the trestle table without being asked, in the same place and the same way as on the third and the fourteenth of this month, and a man of fifty-six said a man who puts a thing in the same place three times is a man who is asking to be asked. He said he had a thing to say that was not for a yard and not for a table and that he wanted it said in the room where the books are, and asked for the man who keeps that road to be asked to leave the yard first. He said in sixty-four words that he went up the road on the twentieth because somebody was going to be at the top of it and he wanted to see whether they were, and that he did not find out and was not going to say he did not find out as though it were a thing he had done. **On the twenty-second, carrying a ledger from the records office to a counter at the end of the lane and back because the man who keeps that counter was not well, he was in that office and read a page in a Registry's hand that carries his own name against this district's records office and says he serves it, entered on the second of April, the day he gave a name at a counter that takes names off sheets. He has carried things for that office nine or ten times and has never been given a job for it and has never been paid a penny of it. He said nothing for three days and then disclosed it himself, in a yard on the twenty-sixth and in a room with a shut door in it on the night of the twenty-fifth.** He said in a hundred and nine words that he did the work and would have carried it anyway and is not a man of that office. He said in twenty-five words: *I thought somebody would take my name off it. I was wrong about that, and I knew it was wrong, and I said it anyway.* He said in fifty-eight words that he has not slept two nights running and that he is not going to say who gave him the sheet and never was. **He loses nothing that was his and pays for saying it: the ledger work stops, a day of his labour is gone, he is no longer a stranger four hundred yards from his own forge, and the name on the page cannot be taken off by him because a name put on a figure cannot be taken off it by the person it is put on, so the saying of it made the name harder to get rid of. A man of fifty-six entered that he counted his own unpaid labour and got nine or ten and that a clerk cannot count another man's days for him.** He was asked in four seconds whether he would go up the road with the answer on the first of the fifth month and said yes and said he was going up anyway, and it was entered that both are a man who has been asked. **He is a man and not a bearer, is not a clerk of a house, is not one of the five things this district does not have, and is not on the standing orders of the nine or on any page of this district's except four lines that do not carry his name.**

## The woman of fifty-eight

**Eleven of about ninety houses still have a door on them, and on the twenty-second of April, asked in his own words and not in the six things, she said in seventy-five words: I have carried water up that bank for nine years and nobody has ever asked me once whether I would rather somebody else carried it, and there is a chair in that sheet and somebody is going to sit in it, and I know which of those two things I have been for nine years, and I am not going to say it in front of nine people on the second day of eleven houses. She picked the two buckets up and went out of the yard and up the bank, about a hundred and forty steps, without stopping, and it was entered that nobody asked her to and that nobody followed her.** On the twenty-fourth she took a bucket off a man who could not lift it and said nothing. On the twenty-seventh she used the yard's own rule about a name said twice, in a hundred and six words, to protect a man who is not hers: *He has been in this yard three days and his name is being said in every yard in four districts and he is not mine and I am not going to be the woman who did it to him.* On the twenty-eighth she refused in thirty-nine words to have a letter about a chair read out to her: *It is a letter about a chair and I have carried water up a hill for nine years and I am not a housekeeper of a water. Write it and send it and do not read it to me.* On the second of the fifth month, uncounted, she said: *I said the name of a yard's rule out loud for a man who is not mine, and I have carried eleven houses for ten days and I have not been thanked for it once and I did not want to be, and that is the first time in nine years a thing I have done has gone unwritten and I find that I do not mind.* **The eleventh bucket went up the bank at about ten past twelve on the second and nobody counted the buckets. Nobody has priced a day. The round of about seventy-nine is not coming back. The offer of four houses from the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale still stands, has not been taken up, and four and eleven were refused entry as an addition on the page.** She is not a protected thing and is not named on the keepers' page and is not a keeper.

## The man with the portfolio

**Still not in this district, off the page, and nothing in this block says where he is or when he comes back.** **The lease was not answered on the last day of the fourth month** and a clerk of nineteen years entered that a lease that has not been answered on the day it falls due has not been refused and has not been taken up and has not been given up, and that the record says not asked, and that she is not going to enter a refusal on a lease nobody in this district was ever asked about. No day was counted for him. He is a competitor and not an antagonist, and this block did not use him and did not cheapen his return.

## The man of about thirty at a counter

**The sixteenth time down the eleven mile road, on the twenty-third of April, a hundred and eleven days after the second of January, which is fifteen weeks and six days. The whole run is named and dated on the page: eleventh on the second, twelfth on the fourth, thirteenth on the sixth, fourteenth on the ninth, fifteenth on the fifteenth, sixteenth today.** He was in that yard about four hours. **He was not asked about the ninth holding on the delivery sheet**, and a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said out loud in ninety-five words that he was not going to, because a man who has been refused seven times in four months has never once been asked why, and because a man put an eighth time in a yard is going to say no again and the eight is going to turn into the ninth for asking and not for refusing. **The pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given stood at EIGHT and did not move, and the refusals about the ninth holding stood at SEVEN and did not move, and the two are not the same figure.** He said in eighty-two words that his house would want a chair before the water did. **He gave four words at a gate on the twenty-third as he was leaving, and nobody heard them, and a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing entered them the next morning: a house at a road nine miles up will let a page be copied and will not let a page be carried.** Asked where the house was, he said he had asked twice in thirty-four miles and had never been given an answer and had stopped asking, and that he was giving them a word and not a name and had never been given a name either. He got nothing out of the four hours and it was entered. **The next is the seventeenth and no chapter fixes a day for it, because this district has never once given that man a day to be here on. He is not a clerk of a house in the sense the refusals count in any other body, is not the holder, is not the bearer, is not on a page, and is not on the keepers' page either.**

## The route keeper

**Walked through that yard at about one in the afternoon on the thirtieth of April without stopping, not sent for, not asked a first thing, and a clerk of nineteen years entered that he has been asked one question in this district since the first of this month and has given one answer and that a man who has been asked one question and given one answer is not a man who has been asked.** He is not a bearer of a road and has never been asked to be one. His eight nights and his refused ninth in forty-two words and his two words that are not on any page are unchanged and off the page in this block.

## The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing

**Copied a page with nine places on it out of a cave in about fifty minutes in a doorway with a lamp on the step, in his own hand, with his left hand on the slate and his right on the pencil because the wind comes round the corner of the lamp every nine or ten minutes and puts the flame over, and said in sixty-six words: It is a doorway and not a room and the lamp is on the step and the wind comes round the corner of it every nine or ten minutes and puts the flame over, and I copied with my left hand on the slate and my right on the pencil, and a man cannot do that for fifty minutes and I did it for fifty minutes. He went home with his hand open and a clerk of nineteen years entered that she was told about it by the man of about thirty-four who digs loam and not by the man it belonged to.** **He would not read the eight names out in the doorway and would not read them out in a yard**, and gave the reason, which is this district's own rule about a name said twice, learned off a woman of fifty-eight in the last month: *he was not going to be the man who used it on eight people who were not in the room.* That is his **third copy in a month** and this district now holds a copy of a stranger's schedule, a copy of the standing orders of the nine and a copy of a page out of a cave, and the three documents nobody owns are three. **Owed eleven coppers a week, refused four times for the same reason, and the fourth refusal for the same reason is not a fourth refusal; the district is still not going to pay him and has now said so four times, and he said on the second of the fifth month that he is not going to say it a fifth.** He said one thing in eighty words that a man of about nineteen counted because he had not said not to, and then said he should not have said not to and was not going to say not to again. He is not a clerk, and a clerk of nineteen years is a clerk, and there are two of those in this basin now and one of them is nineteen.

## The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale

**Asked what a chair is worth for the fourth time in eighteen days and gave the same answer for the fourth time, in sixty-three words: I have weighed nine sacks a day for nine years and I have never weighed a day, and I have never weighed a chair, and a man who wants one of those three priced for him is asking the wrong person, and I have been the wrong person four times now and I am going to stop being asked and start being told.** A clerk entered that a person who gives the same answer four times has not refused four times, and that a person who has not been asked has not refused and cannot be counted either way. **Her four houses still stand, have not been taken up, and were not added to eleven on any of the ten days and are not going to be.** She was in the yard on the twenty-first for ten minutes and went out of the gate without saying anything.

## The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households

**Said in seventy words on the thirtieth of April that the house has to ask him, that he has been asked five times now to be told what to do about the three and has not been told, and that the state of those six has not moved since the twenty-first of last month, which is forty days, and that he would like it written down that five is not a sixth and that a fourth time was not a fifth either.** **The six stand at one signed, two refused, three not asked again.** A man of not asking is not a man who has been refused and is not a count of anything and the record says not asked.

## The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat

**Asked, in the six things, in front of about nineteen people, whether she would carry a ledger two hundred yards each way on the days the man of about nineteen is not well, and said in eighty-three words: I carried one on the twenty-second because it was in my arms and I did not know it was a job, and it was a Friday and I did not know that either, and I have not thought about it once since, and I am not going to say yes to it in a yard and I am not going to say no to it either, and I want it written down that nobody asked me and that I have not said no. A clerk of nineteen years entered that it is the first time in four months that a person in that yard has asked for the not-asking to be written down, and that a request for a thing to be written down is not a column.** She said that the goat is fed at six and does not know what day it is, and that she is not a counter and her name is not on anything. **She is in a pool of tolls with her name not written on anything and has been since the thirtieth of last month, and nobody has asked her about the pool.**

## The woman of about thirty-eight who keeps a building four miles down a lane

**At the end of that lane with a pail of her own on the twenty-fifth and did not come over, and not sent for and not asked a first thing.** She sent word by a boy of about nine on the thirtieth that the beds were in the second room and the water had not come over the step. **A building which has denied nobody entry in nine years has not denied anybody entry on any of the ten days of this block and is not a sixth protected thing.**

## The man of about fifty-five who keeps that building

**The tank is a brick tank about five feet high with a lip about nine inches wide, and it is over its lip, and there are two chalk lines on the inside of the brick, the second above the one he drew on the seventeenth of this month, and the crack is running.** He had been up a ladder with a piece of chalk twice this month and has not told anybody what the lines are for. **He put nine years of beds in the second room of that building at about two in the morning on the twenty-fifth and told nobody until the morning, which is what he said on the twentieth he would do, and asked what he had done said in sixty-six words: I did what I said I would do on the twentieth, at about two in the morning, and I did not tell anybody until the morning, and there are nine years of beds in the second room and I have not lost one of them, and the water is where the water comes from and I am fifty-five and I have stopped expecting to be told.** He said the other half of it unasked and uncounted: **I took the beds up and I left the crack alone and the crack is running and I have not asked anybody for a fifth mend in that tank in four months and I am not going to ask anybody today, and a man who has moved nine years of beds up a stair at two in the morning in the dark is not a man who is going to be told anything about water by a yard.** A refusal with a reason is not one of the refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given, and a man who keeps a building is not a clerk of a house, and the count of eight did not move.

## A man of about nineteen who counts

**The reason every figure in this block is checkable. He counted forty-five sentences in Chapters 221 to 230 resolved from a claim phrase to the sentence printed under it, and nine more counted spans in the form *in N words*, and all fifty-four reproduce.** He was in that yard all day on the twenty-third and had not been asked to do anything, and asked what he counts and why and said he counts because a yard is a place where a number can be checked, and that he did not know why the number mattered, and that he had been counting since the second of April, which is twenty-one days. He was the counter of a man's seven words, a man's fifty-two words, a woman's twenty-eight words, a man's forty-four lines, and of two panels nobody counted. **He is nobody's keeper and is not to be turned into one.**

## A boy of about nine

**Carried four words from the end of a lane four miles down a lower terrace to the yard of Lot Seventeen on the thirtieth of April, and the four words are printed on the page: *The beds are upstairs.* What he had come to say with them was that the beds were in the second room and the water had not come over the step.** A clerk of nineteen years entered that a boy of about nine carried four words and that **a boy of about nine is not a bearer and is not a clerk and is not anybody's instrument**, and that the count of things this district does not have is five and did not move. **He is not the boy whose name is in a column in a building two days of road away and the two are not to be joined.**

## Off the page in this block, and to be left there

**Cael Orin, the leaseholder, whose name was said once out loud in a yard in Chapter 203 and was NOT said a second time in Chapters 211 to 220 and NOT said a third time in Chapters 221 to 230.** Iven, presumed dead from Adrian's point of view throughout, is not named, not referred to and not foreshadowed in any of the ten chapters, and **the ninth design signature belongs to him and the ninth name is not on the page.** The office is in none of them. Lina and Orren are in none of them. **Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block and nothing required him.** The tolls arriving in villages that did not sign, the nine local charters, the regional covenant, the letter calling this district's man a founder on nine walls and the Convener promotion are in none of the ten chapters. **The protected category stays closed at five and no sixth was added, and the block refused a sixth in four chapters.**

---

# Character state after Volume 05 Batch 0004 (Chapters 231 to 240)

**Additive to the sections above, which are the state before this block. Where a figure is named the chapter is the authority.**

## The man the figure of twenty-one years is against

**Wrong twice about a lock, in front of witnesses, and said so out loud both times in his own words.** Chapter 232, in a yard, about nineteen people, ninety-six words: a lock holds a road shut and **what it holds with is the pin, and the pin is the piece that takes the load, and a lock is a safe thing exactly as long as a man is near it.** The man of fifty-six put his hand on the frame next to his and said the pin takes nothing. He then said the whole of what he was wrong about out loud, in a hundred and forty-seven words, and named the reason: a pin is not a wall, a screw is not a wall, a frame is not a wall, and a lock does not need a man to hold it and never did. Chapter 236, on a bank four miles out past the loams, about nine people, ninety-one words: the notch in a sill is the record and a mark like that is a gauge. The man of about forty-one put his thumb in the notch and said it is where the board goes. He then said the whole of it out loud, in a hundred and sixteen words: **I read a mark as a job.** A clerk entered each time that neither saying is a figure and neither is a column. **The cost was public: a man of about thirty-four who mends fencing proposed the whole behaviour as a seventh instrument of this district in ninety-seven words and the clerk refused it in about four seconds, and the man who proposed it came back on the twelfth and said out loud that a rule about being wrong is the same shape as the rule about being thanked that one of the nine wrote four hundred years ago.**

**He said out loud in a yard on the eighth, in eighty-nine words, that he does not know what to do about four places and a notice and a pin, and a man of fifty-six answered that a man who says that twice in a month is not a man without a plan.** On the twelfth he said what he is going to do, in a hundred and two words: **he will ask the two places that have not answered, each of them separately, in their own doorway, about the thing they have not answered, and will not take the other two with him and will not ask one about the other, and will not go up there with a sheet and a chair and a body that wants a man.** In a room on the ninth he put both of his errors on her table himself. In about nine feet of lane on the eleventh he said out loud the fortnight of silence about a question he had asked her on the twenty-fourth of last month, and she said he did it on purpose, and he asked the other half of it, and on the twelfth she came. **He is not a bearer of a toll, is not a holder, is not a founder, is not a party of the fourth of four, and is not on the standing orders of the nine.**

## The registrar of this district's records office

**Identified by a document for the first time in this district, and identified without a name. This is the block's political cost and it is on the page in a hundred and ten words and it is not a comfort.** Asked in the six things in front of about nineteen people in Chapter 234, she said: a rival record is not a finding and not a truth and not a charge, and **my name is not on that notice. Nobody's name is on that notice. I have read it three times to be certain and there is not one name in it.** And then: **the only line in that notice that has anything to do with me is the second one, and the second one is my own rule, and I wrote it on the twenty-first of the fourth month, and I wrote it before I knew there was a page in my office with a man's name on it or a chair on a table in this yard or a word printed four times on a piece of paper. A book is kept in one room by one person and that person is the only one who may look in it. That is my rule. It is the only rule anybody in this district has ever written down and enforced, and I enforce it, and I have turned people away from that door with it. It is also, this morning, the only reason that notice could be written.** And then: **And nobody has put my name on anything, so I cannot stand in this yard this morning and say that a name put on a figure cannot be taken off it by the person it is put on, which is this yard's rule and a good one and which I would like very much to have today. What is on that notice is not my name. It is my room, and my books, and the fact that I am the only one who may look in them, and all three of those are true, and I wrote two of them myself.** She refused a fifth condition with no end on it in eighty-four words, on the ground that a body can be held to a condition and this district has nobody in it to be held. **She disagreed with a panel out loud in a yard on the ninth, in ninety words, that a notice affects a person before the day it is effective on, and said of its third line that a body which has decided in advance it will not need a name has decided that in advance, and the decision is printed, and the decision is the finding.** She refused the man the figure of twenty-one years is against a copy of the notice going into a book, in a room with a shut door in it, and it is not in a book. **On the twelfth she walked four miles and put her thumb in a notch in a sill and said, in a hundred and four words, that it is a hole a plank fits in, that somebody cut it four hundred years before her, that her name is not anywhere near it and her room is not anywhere near it, and that she wrote the rule that made her room findable and would not write it a second time differently.** The last exchange between them in this block is **You did not tell anybody at that yard that I said I would come. / No. / Good. I would not have come.** She is not a bearer, is not a holder, is not a party of the fourth of four, and is not a clerk of anything.

## The man of about thirty-four who digs loam

**The body cost in this block is his and it is in scenes and not in a footer.** He carried a barrow with about a hundred and ninety pounds on it up a bank in about eleven minutes at the near shaft on the fourth of the fifth month and told the yard he was fine at the bottom of it and was not, and told the truth about that on the tenth, in a hundred and fifty-two words, having said fine because it was nine people and a barrow. He put his hand into a channel to feel the bottom of a sill on the eighth because a man he had met once asked him to, and **was not in the yard at all on the ninth**, and came back on the tenth with a sling the woman of fifty-eight lent him and that nobody asked her for. He got it up off the ground without the two goes the second had needed and said, uncounted, that he does not know whether that is good news or a thing that gets worse later. **He disagreed with a panel out loud in Chapter 233 in a hundred and four words, and that is the second time in the volume he has done it.** He refused the join of two records a fifth, sixth, seventh, eighth and ninth time in five chapters, and a clerk entered each time that the number of people watching it is not a reason. **He said the finding of the block in eighty-nine words: a lock is the only instrument in this basin that has never needed a person, and he added that he is not going to use this yard's rule about people on a piece of iron because the rule is about people and iron is not people, and that the honest way to say what has happened for forty days is that nine structures in the water above four districts have been standing open on eight of eleven nights and nobody in this district has been to look at one of them.** Asked one question about it in his own words he said, in ninety-nine words, that he could have walked to one of them in a morning and any of them could have. **On the twelfth he said out loud that he was the one who proposed the seventh instrument and did not want to be the one who took it back.**

## The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing

Named the borrowed word on the third of the fifth month before anybody asked and distinguished it from the gate at the end of that yard in the same sentence. Said out loud that he is not going to be the man who decides that this district is the fourth of the four, in seventy-nine words, so that if somebody does decide it there is a line in the book saying a man noticed. Refused the join of the two fours three times in three chapters. Refused a fifth column of not-askings himself in ninety-five words. **Proposed the seventh instrument in ninety-seven words and had it refused in about four seconds.** **On the eleventh he walked two hundred yards up behind the bank, asked the woman of fifty-eight for leave first, lifted the hasp on a plank door that has not a handle on it, and put one question to one man in his own doorway, and wrote down what he said and did not argue with it.** Read the notice out in four chapters, five times in seven days in all. He is in nine of the ten chapters and has not been asked a first question about his own body in any of them.

## The man of about fifty-six

**He is the one who is right about the pin, in a hundred and twelve words, with his own hand on the frame next to the other man's hand, out of nine years of putting a stick through that pin about four thousand times with both hands in the water.** He is in eight of the ten chapters, gives the figures out loud, reads the board, and is not a clerk and has said so about himself about a dozen times in four months. He said that a rule about being thanked and a rule about being wrong are the same shape of rule, and that a man who has been asked one question in nine years and has answered it is the only instrument this basin has that the four districts have not built yet.

## The man of about forty-one who keeps a road house at the top of the road

**New speaking character in this block and the one who is right about the notch, in eighty words, with his thumb in it.** He came down the only road in this district that climbs on the fifth of the fifth month, nine miles with the last half mile a goat track along a shoulder, in about four hours, and was in the yard from about half past eleven, and said his house is the fourth of the four and that his house did not sign. He said, in eighty-four words: **The party of a house is a house. There is nobody in our house who has been asked to pay anything and I am not going to be the first one they ask, because the day they ask me is the day the house has signed without anybody going into the room. My answer is the same as the one your woman of fifty-eight gave about her two buckets, and I have not heard her say it and I have not read it out.** He said, in twenty-three words, that a name over a door is a name on a house and not a name on a figure, and that he has never in forty years been asked to hold anything and would know. He was at the channel on the eighth when the sill was read and said the notch is a cut for the edge of a plank and the board is not there because it is May. **He is not a keeper, has never been asked to be one, is not a bearer, is not on a page, has no day in this district, and no chapter may give him one.** He was not there on the twelfth and nobody was sent for him.

## The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing

**Refused two things in this block with a reason and neither refusal moved the pool of eight.** He would not copy the toll sheet, in eighty words, because a district that owns a toll sheet is a district that is arguing about the toll. He would not draw the lock, in sixty-five words, because there are nine of them and not one is ours. He said in eighty-nine words that his hand closes about halfway now and that he said he was not going to say it a fifth time and is not, and that somebody asked him about it and he answered, and that those are two different things. He made a fourth copy, of the notice, in about twenty minutes, in his own hand, for nothing. **Owed eleven coppers a week, refused five times for the same reason, and the fifth refusal for the same reason is not a fifth refusal; the district is still not going to pay him.**

## The woman of fifty-eight

**Three reactions and one consent in this block.** She refused, in seventy-two words, to have a toll put on her two buckets: *I carry water to eleven houses and I have never been asked to carry it for anybody's gate and I am not going to be asked now. If a body wants my two buckets it can come up this bank and say so to my face and I will put them down, and until it does there is a woman on this bank and not a party of the fourth of four.* **A clerk entered that a refusal with a reason is not one of the eight, and that a woman holding eleven houses of a round of about ninety and no figure on them at all is a person and not a party of anything.** She said yes in about four seconds, with a reason, in seventy-two words, to a man going to ask a question at the ninth of her eleven houses, on the ground that if he asked it in a yard she would take the bucket off him in front of everybody. She said one thing about a sling, uncounted, that a yard in this basin has four things a person is asked about and none of them is a piece of cloth. She lent a sling on the tenth that nobody asked her to. **The twelfth bucket went up the bank and nobody counted the buckets. Nobody has priced a day. She is not a protected thing and is not named on the keepers' page and is not a keeper.**

## The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale

**Stood in the yard on the ninth for the first time in a fortnight and said the first thing anybody has done in four months that was any use, in a hundred and nine words: the reason four houses are standing empty is that nobody has asked anybody in them a single question, and if a body four hundred miles away is going to start asking places questions in three weeks then the first thing anybody in this basin has to do is go and ask somebody.** **Her four houses still stand, have not been taken up, and were not added to eleven on any of the ten days.**

## The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households

Said out loud on the seventh, in a hundred and six words, that nobody had asked him anything that morning and that he was going to stop, and named the sixteen days the six have not moved, and said that five is not a sixth. On the ninth, in a hundred and seven words, he said that a body four hundred miles away asking four places a question is a body asking and not this yard, and that his six will know the difference because he has told them the difference, and that he is not going to work out which of his six is in which of the four. **The six stand at one signed, two refused, three not asked again.**

## The man of about fifty-five who keeps a building four miles down a lane

Said, in eighty-five words, that the crack has gone about the length of his hand since the twenty-fifth of last month, that it is not going to close, that he has not asked anybody for a mend in that tank in four months and is not going to ask anybody today, and that the second room still has nine years of beds in it and the water has not come over the step since he moved them. **The tank is over its lip, there are two chalk lines on the inside of the brick, and the crack is about the length of his hand. He sent nothing on the tenth and nobody sent anything to him. A building which has denied nobody entry in nine years has not denied anybody entry on any of the ten days of this block and is not a sixth protected thing.**

## The man of about forty-four at the ninth of the eleven houses

**Asked one question in nine years on the eleventh of the fifth month and answered it.** He was sitting by his own door with a mended sleeve and no coat on, and the door is a plank door with a hasp and not a handle. He refused a toll with a reason — *No. And I am not going to tell you what I think about the four places, either, and I do not know which of the four we are, and I have not got a copy of it and I am not going to get one* — and said that if anybody came from this yard and asked him a second thing he would answer that too. **A man who keeps a house is not a clerk of a house and the eight did not move. He is not a bearer and is not on a page.**

## A man of about nineteen who counts

**Counted fifty-four sentences in Chapters 231 to 240 and all fifty-four reproduce.** He is in that yard every morning. On the ninth he said, in sixty-eight words, that it was the first morning since the second of April that he had counted nothing before about eleven, and that he did not know what he would count instead. He counted nothing in about four minutes on a channel bank in Chapter 236 because there was nothing to count and he was not asked to count something instead. **He is nobody's keeper and is not to be turned into one.**

## The route keeper

Walked through the yard on the tenth of the fifth month without stopping, not sent for, not asked a first thing, and has been asked one question in this district since the first of the month and has given one answer. On the eighth he walked out to the top field with nine people and turned back at the field gate and went home, and nobody asked him where he was going. **He is not a bearer of a road and has never been asked to be one.**

## The man with the portfolio

**Still not in this district, off the page, and nothing in this block says where he is or when he comes back.** No day was counted for him. He is a competitor and not an antagonist, and this block did not use him and did not cheapen his return.

## The man of about thirty at a counter

**Not in this district on any of the ten days of this block, and no chapter gave him a day.** He is at the sixteenth time down the eleven mile road and the next is the seventeenth, and the refusals about the ninth holding are seven and did not move, and the pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given is eight and did not move against four separate attempts. **He is not a clerk of a house in the sense the refusals count in any other body, is not the holder, is not the bearer, is not on a page, and is not on the keepers' page either.**

## Off the page in this block, and to be left there

**Cael Orin, the leaseholder, whose name was said once out loud in a yard in Chapter 203 and was NOT said a second time in Chapters 211 to 230 and NOT said a second or a third time in Chapters 231 to 240.** Iven, presumed dead from Adrian's point of view throughout, is not named, not referred to and not foreshadowed in any of the ten chapters, **and the ninth design signature belongs to him and the ninth name is not on the page and the ninth place is ruled and empty.** The office is in none of them. Lina and Orren are in none of them. **Selik Marne is a wary ally and not a friend and is not in this block and nothing required him.** The nine local charters, the regional covenant, the letter calling this district's man a founder on nine walls and the Convener promotion are in none of the ten chapters. **The protected category stays closed at five and no sixth was added, and the block refused a sixth in two chapters and refused a fifth instrument in three and refused a seventh instrument in one.**
