# Character state after Volume 05 Batch 0005 (Chapters 241 to 250)

**Additive to the sections above. This is the last block of Volume 05. The figures below are the state at Chapter 250 and the volume close inherits them.**

## The man the figure of twenty-one years is against

**HE IS A CONVENER, GRANTED BY SHOW OF HANDS IN A BARN ABOUT NINE MILES UP THE ONLY ROAD IN THIS DISTRICT THAT CLIMBS, ON THE TWENTIETH OF THE FIFTH MONTH, AND THE PROMOTION IS NOT A SCREEN.** He said the price out loud in a hundred and forty-nine words before he took it: a name in a column in a book a stranger may walk up to, which is a worse door than a page. **A name put on a figure cannot be taken off it by the person it is put on, and he said that rule himself and did not ask anybody to take it off.** **A stranger stood at the gate of that yard at ten past seven on the twentieth and asked for him by his name instead of asking for a page, four miles on the strength of the name, and he answered from about nine feet away without being fetched and was carrying a bucket.** He said on the fourteenth and the eighteenth what the two places that had not answered said, in their own words, in their own doorways, on his own two feet. He was not a founder, is not a holder, is not a bearer of a toll, is not a party of the fourth of four, and is not on the standing orders of the nine. He is a person carrying four words in an appeal that goes by its own road into another region.

## The registrar of this district's records office

**SHE IS NOT A BEARER, NOT A HOLDER, NOT A STEWARD AND NOT A PARTY OF ANYTHING, AND SHE SAID SO ALOUD BEFORE SHE SIGNED ANYTHING.** She refused to sign the first charter, in a hundred and forty-one words, on the ground that a registrar who signs a charter has put her room behind somebody's arrangement. She signed **one line out of six** of the covenant and told a barn of about nineteen people which one and why, in a hundred and thirty-nine words. She put the covenant into the book in her room and nowhere else, on the ground that a stranger may walk up and read it there, and that none of the nine local charters goes in there. **On the twenty-first of the fifth month she asked to read the Registry's notice out in a yard of about nineteen people and got through the first line and stopped at the second, which is her own rule of the twenty-first of last month, and nobody in that yard said anything to her about it.** Her rule is unchanged and the notice that takes effect on the first of the next month does not change a word of it.

## The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing

**THE BLOCK'S INSTRUMENT, AND HE PAID FOR IT IN HIS LEFT WRIST.** At about half past ten on the fifteenth he put his left hand on a wire above a two-foot drop on a goat track to get past a man with two buckets, and it took the skin off the inside of his wrist about the length of a thumb, and he did not stop and did not say anything. He wore his cuff buttoned over it for four days, could not close his hand on a fence post at a field gate at about half past three on the nineteenth, and on the twentieth a man of about thirty-four who digs loam tied the wire for him in about four seconds and said nothing about it either. He asked the questions in the doorways and the rooms, said the whole method of the nine out loud in a yard in a hundred and forty words so that a clerk could write it down, read the notice out in that yard seven times in seventeen days, read the covenant out in a barn standing on a bin, carried the letter up the road that climbs and put it into nine things that were not envelopes, and carried the slate past a kitchen for the twelfth time and did not read out what was on it. **He is not a founder and did not deny the word when the letter came.**

## The man of about thirty-four who digs loam

**HE ENDED THE VOLUME STANDING UP TO THE KNEE IN A CHANNEL AT THE SLUICE END OF THE LOAMS WITH HIS HAND ON NOTHING, AND THE FRAME HELD THE WATER BECAUSE A FRAME HOLDS WATER AND THE WEIGHT OF WATER SITS ON A FRAME.** His right arm was out of the sling for the first time since the seventh of the fifth month on the fourteenth; it came out of the sling at the near shaft on the fourth of the month, when he said he was fine and was not, and he put his hand into a channel on the eighth because a man he had met once asked him to, and he lost a day out of the yard on the ninth of the month before that. **He refused three joins in three chapters, disagreed with a panel out loud in a barn in a hundred and seventeen words, and said the cost of a right to withdraw in two speeches in a hundred and twenty-five words and a hundred and fifty-nine words, and is not one of the five and does not live in any of their rooms and nobody asked him what a withdrawal would cost them.** He said the finding on the fifteenth in a hundred and fifty-three words and the month's finding on the twenty-second in two hundred and six words. **A rule about being wrong and a rule about being thanked are the same shape of rule and he has not said so again since the twelfth of last month and must not.**

## The man of fifty-six

**HE IS RIGHT AND HE SAYS SO WITH NO PLEASURE IN IT.** He disagreed with the third line of the second panel out loud in a yard in a hundred and twenty-six words: a figure is a thing, a thing cannot say no, and a person can, and the reason a toll can be fastened to a person is exactly that the person can say no to it. He said that nine people wrote that sentence four hundred years ago to stop a man exactly like this one, and said out loud that a sheet in a yard is not a page in a cave and that he is not going to say they are the same instrument. He gave the count of what the month had got in a hundred and thirty-seven words. **He is not a clerk and has said so about himself about a dozen times in four months and a day a man does not refuse is not a day he has agreed to anything.**

## The man of about forty-one who keeps a road house at the top of the road

**HE IS NOT A KEEPER AND HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED TO BE ONE, AND NO KEEPER HAS EVER BEEN ON A PAGE AND THERE NEVER WAS ONE.** He said, in a hundred and fifty-six words, that he did not put the board in the notch and his father did not, that somebody comes in March and goes down again before the water is down, that he does not know what he is called and has not asked, and that the day he asks is the day there is a man at the top of that road instead of a house, and this house has never had a man in it. **He has no day in this district and none may be given to him, and he was standing outside his own door at about ten past seven on the fifteenth and the twentieth and was not asked a first thing on either morning.**

## The man of about forty-four at the ninth of the eleven houses

**HE WALKED TWO HUNDRED YARDS DOWN BEHIND THE BANK ON HIS OWN TWO FEET, WAS ASKED A SECOND QUESTION, AND SAID HE WOULD WANT HIS OWN HOUSE'S ANSWER IN THERE AND ALSO THAT HE DID NOT KNOW WHAT WAS IN IT.** His two halves are in the record and not in one column. **He is the first name under a line in this district, and he put it there himself in his own room with somebody in the door.** He is a man who keeps a house and is not a clerk of a house.

## The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing

**HE IS STILL NOT BEING PAID AND THE DISTRICT HAS NOW SAID SO SEVEN TIMES.** He refused two things in this block with a reason and neither refusal moved the eight: a copy of a thing the district had made up that morning, in eighty-four words, and a copy of the ninth sheet, in a hundred and eight words, the second because a copy of an absence is a thing that travels. He asked for eleven coppers three times, was refused three times, and made the covenant copy and the letter copy anyway, for nothing. His right hand does not close on a cold morning and he has stopped telling people.

## The woman of fifty-eight

**SHE HOLDS ELEVEN HOUSES AND NO FIGURE AND HER BUCKETS ARE ABOUT ELEVEN POUNDS FULL AND NOBODY HAS EVER ASKED HER.** She said it uncounted on the seventeenth. She read the covenant back in a barn on the nineteenth after saying yes in four seconds. **The sling she lent the man of about thirty-four who digs loam has not been asked for back and she has not said she wants it, and the record says not asked.** She would not let her eleven houses be a ninth charter and would not let her round be counted. Nobody counted the buckets on any of the ten days.

## The man of about fifty-five who keeps a building four miles down a lane

**THE TANK IS STILL ABOUT A HAND'S BREADTH OVER ITS LIP, THE TWO CHALK LINES ARE STILL ON THE INSIDE OF THE BRICK, THE CRACK IS ABOUT THE LENGTH OF HIS HAND, THE NINE YEARS OF BEDS ARE STILL IN THE SECOND ROOM, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED FOR A FIFTH MEND IN FOUR MONTHS AND COUNTING.** He refused to be a steward for a season before he was asked, in a hundred and twenty-nine words, and asked for what he said to be read back to him. He sent nothing to that yard on the twenty-second and nobody sent anything to him. A crack in a tank is not a sixth protected thing.

## The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale

**HER FOUR HOUSES STILL STAND AND WERE NOT ADDED TO ELEVEN AND WERE NOT OFFERED AGAIN.** The age was never in dispute and is thirty-six throughout. **The sex was wrong: Volume 05 made her a man in twenty-nine passages, the close did not examine it, and the review of the close found it and repaired it. Closed canon is a woman, in Chapters 141, 146, 151, 164, 165, 173, 177, 184, 187, 195 and 199, and a Volume 05 writer may not make her a man again.** She gave the yard nothing and said no before she was asked, in a hundred and five words, and said she would read the record back at the counter herself.

## The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households

**HIS SIX STAND AT ONE SIGNED, TWO REFUSED AND THREE NOT ASKED AGAIN AND HAVE NOT MOVED SINCE THE TWENTY-FIRST OF LAST MONTH, WHICH IS THIRTY-ONE DAYS AT THE LAST DAY OF THE VOLUME, DERIVED. The printed figures are twenty-three on the fourteenth of the fifth month, twenty-six on the seventeenth, twenty-eight on the nineteenth and twenty-nine on the twentieth, and NO CHAPTER IN CHAPTERS 241 TO 250 PRINTS THIRTY-ONE. He is a man of about forty-eight in all fifty chapters and always was: the close changed fourteen passages the wrong way, to thirty-four, and the review changed them back, because the closed canon of Chapters 146, 164, 166, 173, 179, 184, 187, 195 and 196 and of the Volume 03 and Volume 04 rolls is a man of about forty-eight and he says himself in Chapter 184 that he is fifty-one.** He asked whether his six were one of the nine so that he would not have to lie about it later, and he said he was glad of a thing that was not on the paper, and a hand put up for one reason is not a hand put up for another.

## The man of about thirty-four who keeps a road

**HE IS NOT A BEARER OF THE ROAD AND HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED TO BE ONE.** He carried a lamp nobody asked him to carry, waited at a field gate nobody asked him to wait at, went past the field gate on the fifteenth when he had turned back on the eighth, brought a fifth sheet down the road that climbs inside his coat without being asked to fetch it, and carried the letter up. He has been asked one question in this district since the first of this month and has given one answer.

## A man of about nineteen who counts

**HE COUNTED FIFTY-THREE SENTENCES IN CHAPTERS 241 TO 250 AND ALL FIFTY-THREE REPRODUCE.** On the fifteenth, on a shoulder in the cold, he counted nine men going up and nine coming off and did not count the board or the water or the four generations, and said that if he had put a figure on it that afternoon he would have been the first man in this basin who made one up. He counts because a yard is a room with no roof and a number can be checked in it.

## The man of about thirty at a counter

**NOT IN THIS DISTRICT ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS OF THIS BLOCK, AND NO CHAPTER GAVE HIM A DAY.** He is at the sixteenth time down the eleven mile road and the next is the seventeenth, and the refusals about the ninth holding are seven and are a different figure from the eight.

## The man with the portfolio

**STILL NOT IN THIS DISTRICT, OFF THE PAGE, AND NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK SAYS WHERE HE IS.** The lease is unanswerable and he is a competitor and not an antagonist.

## Off the page in this block, and to be left there

**Cael Orin, whose name was said once out loud in a yard in Chapter 203 and was not said a second or a third time in Chapters 211 to 250. Iven Tallow, the ninth design signature, who is not in the volume, does not appear, and is not to be named or foreshadowed by the volume close. Selik Marne, a wary ally and not a friend, who is not in this block and nothing required him. The nine keepers of the four generations, none of whom has ever been on a page. The Registrar-General, whose name is on a lease and on a portfolio nobody in this district has read.**

---

# Character state after Volume 06 Batch 0001 (Chapters 251 to 260)

**Additive to every section above. Volume 06 is open. The figures below are the state at Chapter 260 and Batch 0002 inherits them.**

## The man the figure of twenty-one years is against

**HE SAID THE VOLUME'S SENTENCE ABOUT HIMSELF OUT LOUD, IN A YARD, ON THE TENTH OF THE SIXTH MONTH: A door is a thing you build for the people who come to it, and I have built nine, and the only people who have come to them are the ones who already had a reason to come.** He is a Convener and the promotion is not a screen and the price was paid on the twentieth of the fifth month and is still being paid. He offered a woman of about thirty-one who keeps a route a sheet with her name under a line at six in the morning without asking her first what she was doing that month, was refused in about four seconds on his own rule said back to him, and said in a hundred and eighty-three words that he had watched a door cost him the last private thing he had and had built one anyway. He asked the registrar for the number of names in the second book in her own doorway and was refused in about four seconds. He counted forty-one lines off two open pages of her book at four in the morning on the seventh with his own finger and was not asked and did not ask, and she has known since and has not said anything about it in a yard. **He is not a founder, not a holder, not a bearer of a toll, not a party of the fourth of four, not on the standing orders of the nine, and findable.** **A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX TOLD HIM THAT STANDING NEXT TO A THING IS NOT STANDING IN ITS PLACE AND HE HAS NOT ACTED ON IT AND THE LESSON IS STATED ONCE.**

## The registrar of this district's records office

**SHE OPENED A SECOND BOOK IN THE ROOM WHERE THE BOOKS ARE AND SAID OUT LOAD THAT SHE HAD PUT HER OWN ROOM BEHIND SOMEBODY'S ARRANGEMENT.** The book is a bound ledger of the same make as the first, about three-quarters of an inch thick, on the shelf on its side with a second chair turned up on top of it. It is in her hand, in one room, and nobody else has looked in it and nobody else will look in it. **Her rule of the twenty-first of the fourth month says a book is kept in one room by one person and that person is the only one who may look in it, and that rule is the only reason a notice four hundred miles away could be written and the only reason a covenant can be in a book a stranger may walk up to, and she broke it and said she knew what she was doing at about five in the morning.** **She refused the man the figure of twenty-one years a number in her own doorway in about four seconds and then said, in the same doorway, that she was not going to be the second person in his book this week who hands him a number and calls it help. On the ninth she disagreed with a printed rule out loud in a room with a shut door in it, to him and not to a room, and it was not counted, and the record about it came from him and not from her.** On the tenth, over a slate, she said: do not come and find me about the forty-one. **She is not a bearer, not a holder, not a steward and not a party of anything, and a name put on a figure cannot be taken off it by the person it is put on is the rule that protects her as much as it catches him and she says so. She was asleep at her desk at about four in the morning on the seventh and her right hand would not close on a pen for about nine seconds. The building with two doors is not in her book and is not in any book in this district and is not going in one, and she said out loud in a yard that she is not a keeper of it and has never been asked to be.**

## The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing

**HE PAID WITH HIS LEFT HAND TWICE IN TEN DAYS.** He could not get his fingers round the nail in a hasp on the fifth and it took him about nine seconds, and on the first the gate would not open and it took him four goes with his left hand and a fifth with his right, and on the second he did not try it again. **He refused to read the rival record out an eighth time, in about four seconds, with a reason, on the morning it took effect, and no one in that yard argued with him about it.** He asked the questions in the doorways, asked the woman of fifty-eight about her sleep, offered the man of about thirty-four who digs loam a sheet with a fold still in it and was refused, asked the woman of about thirty-one whether the route passed the frame and was refused with a reason and did not ask a second thing, asked who wrote a sheet with three lines and no name on it, and carried the slate past that kitchen for the thirteenth time and wrote one line on it and did not read it out. **He said in a yard that he had been wrong about five months of work and had found it out at the back of a building.** He is not a founder and did not deny the word when the letter came in Volume 05, and he is not a keeper of anything.

## The man of about thirty-four who digs loam

**HE ENDED THE BLOCK ON A SUBTRACTION HE REFUSED TO CALL A FINDING.** He did the sum of the knock out loud in a hundred and seventeen words. He refused the same join a fourth time in a hundred and fifty-nine words and the thirteen and the twelve did not move, and he said in a yard that a join is a join and a fourth refusal of one join is one join with four days of people watching it. **He disagreed with the first panel out loud in a hundred and forty-four words and his objection was not to the rule and was to the third line: you do not write it down by asking a figure to be a thing about people.** He put his right hand flat on the sill under about a foot and a half of water on the second and could not close it for the rest of the afternoon, and had not said so and said nothing on the third. He found the registrar asleep at her desk at four in the morning on the seventh and held a board for the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing without being asked. He is not one of the five and does not live in any of their rooms and nobody has asked him what a withdrawal would cost them.

## The man of fifty-six

**HE SAID THE VOLUME'S SENTENCE AND SAID WHY HE WAS SAYING IT TO THAT MAN RATHER THAN TO A YARD: I am not going to be a man who takes a thing off a man who has just found it out on his own.** He said his own rule is worse than a printed one because a printed one needs nobody in the room. He derived the removal day out loud from the first of the month and not off a sheet. He looked at a building eleven miles off from a rise for about nine minutes and said one sentence about the difference between not being frightened of it and not wanting to ask anybody anything in eleven miles. He refused eleven coppers a week for the eighth time. **He disagreed with the third line of the second panel out loud and then said nothing else at all, and a clerk entered that a man who says he disagrees and does not say why has not converted a disagreement into a finding.** He is right and says so with no pleasure in it and a man of fifty-six is not a clerk and has said so about himself about a dozen times in four months.

## The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale

**SHE IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX AND SHE HAS STOOD AT THAT SCALE NINE YEARS AND WEIGHS ABOUT NINE SACKS A DAY, AND SHE IS THE PERSON WHO ASKED THE QUESTION THAT EXPOSED A BORROWED WORD AND SHE SUPPLIED NEITHER WORD: IN CHAPTER 205 SHE HEARD OPEN READ OUT FOUR TIMES IN A YARD AND ASKED WHAT WAS OPENING, AND A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX SAID OUT LOUD THAT THE DISTRICT HAD BEEN USING THE REGISTRY'S WORD FOR FOUR DAYS WITHOUT KNOWING IT AND THAT THE ASKING WAS DONE BY A WOMAN AT A WEIGHBRIDGE. **THE CLOSED TWO-WORDS CANON IS THAT *gates* IS THE REGISTRY'S WORD AND IS THE WORD ON THE STRANGER'S SCHEDULE, AND *locks* IS THE KEEPERS' WORD AND CAME FROM THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY AT A COUNTER IN CHAPTER 209, WHO SAID HIS HOUSE HAS CALLED THEM LOCKS SINCE BEFORE ANYBODY LIVING WAS BORN. THE EARLIER VERSION OF THIS LINE, WHICH SAID SHE GOT THE KEEPERS' WORD OUT OF A STRANGER'S SCHEDULE, WAS WRONG ON BOTH COUNTS AND IS WITHDRAWN.**** The age was never in dispute and the sex is canon and a Volume 06 writer may not make her a man. **Her four houses still stand, were not added to eleven, were not taken, and she refused them again before she was asked on the first of this month, and said she would read the record back at the counter herself and this time there would be two of them reading it.** She stood at that scale from about six in the morning until about half past five on the fourth, weighed about nineteen sacks instead of about nine because nine people who had walked four miles in the night brought their own, and could not get her right arm above her shoulder afterwards. **On the seventh she said out loud in a room with nothing over the door that she was going to sit at that scale on the eleventh of this month whether anybody was there or not.** A bucket of hers is about eleven pounds full and **nobody counted the buckets on any of the ten days and a clerk entered that nobody did.**

## The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households

**HE IS A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT AND NOT THIRTY-FOUR.** Volume 05 said thirty-four in fourteen chapters and the Volume 05 close changed three of them the wrong way and the review of the close changed all fourteen back, because **the closed canon of Chapters 146, 164, 166, 173, 179, 184, 187, 195 and 196 and of the Volume 03 and Volume 04 rolls is a man of about forty-eight and he says himself in Chapter 184 that he is fifty-one.** His six stand at one signed, two refused and three not asked again and have not moved since the twenty-first of the fourth month: **the printed figures are forty-one on the first of this month, forty-four on the fourth, forty-seven on the seventh and fifty on the tenth, and on the fourth and the seventh and the tenth he said out loud that he was going to say it once and not again this month.** A man who keeps a count of six households is not a clerk of a house and is not one of the eight.

## The man of about thirty-four who keeps a road

**HE IS A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR AND HE KEEPS A ROAD AND HE IS NOT THE TALLY KEEPER AND HE IS NOT THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM AND HE IS NOT THE MAN WHO MENDS FENCING. Three different men and one woman in four trades inside four hundred yards of each other, and no two of them may be merged and the trade is the identifier and it never carries the identification alone.** He refused a second thing in a yard on the grounds that he has been asked one question in this district since the first of the month that is now last month and has given one answer, and that if this yard wants to know how many doors there are he will walk them and will not write them down. He went out on the hidden routes in front of about nine people at night without being asked. **He said on a rise that there is a gate on his four miles about a mile before the place where the ground stops and everybody thinks it is the far end of the road, and that the road goes on another seven miles which he has not walked in six years, and that nobody has ever asked him what is at the end of it and he would like that written down as a thing nobody has asked him.** He is not a bearer of the road and has never been asked to be one.

## The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat

**SHE IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-THREE OR THIRTY-FOUR AND SHE IS AT THE THIRD HOUSE UP THE LANE FROM THE SCALE.** Volume 05's canon card had her as a man of about thirty-six and the prose has a woman and the card is corrected. **She held a slate for nine minutes on the fourth of this month for a woman who could not lift her arm and could not get her hand round the edge of it at the end, and said no to a second thing before she was asked, in a printed span of twelve words: I AM NOT A SECOND HAND AND I WILL NOT BE ONE** She has kept a goat for eleven years and has held a bucket and a rope and a kid and nothing else heavier than about nine pounds since the spring. She is not a clerk of a house and not a keeper of a slate.

## The woman of about thirty-one who keeps a route for people who are not in any book

**NEW TO VOLUME 06, AND THE TRADE IS A TRADE AND NOT A NAME.** She came down four miles in the dark on the third of this month, the first time anybody in this district has seen her in eleven months, and refused a name under a line in about four seconds. **SHE IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THIS BASIN WHO HAS EVER FOUND HIM WITHOUT BEING SENT, AND SHE IS HIS SISTER, AND THAT FACT IS ON NO PAGE IN THIS DISTRICT AND HAS NEVER BEEN ON ONE.** She refused to say how many people there are and said the record would say not asked from the person best placed to answer it. She took the skin off the inside of her left shin about the length of two fingers on the wire on the left of the lane behind the bank on the fourth, walked four miles back with it, said nothing about it and did not limp. She walked about nine people out on the routes at night in fours and fives. She said the rope on the bell frame was untied at the top and pulled down a bit at a time and that whoever took it wanted the bell there and unreachable. She said the route does not pass the frame. **She is not a bearer of a route, has never been asked to be one, and is not a keeper.**

## The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room with nothing over the door

**NEW TO VOLUME 06.** She has kept a room about nine feet by fourteen behind a building about two miles the other way for four years, and there is nothing over the door. The room trades in three things and a clerk entered the three and did not enter a fourth, and about eleven notches are cut in the underside of a bench at intervals that are not even and are not a calendar and are not a count of nights and are not a count of people. **She said no before she was asked to the fifth of the five things this district does not have and refused a list of nine houses in her own room by proxy, and the reason she gave for the list is that the man who copies for nothing would not have sold it and that is why he is not to have it.** She is not a clerk of a house and not a bearer of a bench.

## The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing

**HE IS STILL NOT BEING PAID AND THE DISTRICT HAS NOW SAID NO EIGHT TIMES.** He asked for eleven coppers a week for the eighth time in this block, in Chapter 255, and was refused with a reason. **HE MADE NO COPY OF THE REMOVAL SHEET AND NO COPY OF ANYTHING AT ALL.** He was refused a copy of a sheet with nothing on it and a list of nine houses, both with reasons, and both times the reason was that he would not have sold what he was given. A man who copies for nothing is not a clerk of a house and a refusal with a reason is not one of the eight.

## The woman of fifty-eight

**SHE HOLDS ELEVEN HOUSES AND NO FIGURE AND HER BUCKETS ARE ABOUT ELEVEN POUNDS FULL AND NOBODY COUNTED THEM ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT NOBODY DID.** She told the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing that the ninth of the eleven houses she carries to has a woman in it who is being moved on the first of the month after next. **On the fourth of this month she was at the top of the bank at about ten minutes to four in the morning with a lamp and a bucket, because she comes down at six most mornings and had come down at four, and she saw the nine of them come the last two hundred yards and put the lamp down and picked it up again and said nothing at all.** She took a pail off a man whose hand would not close and did not ask him about it. The sling she lent the man who digs loam has still not been asked for back and she has not said she wants it, and the record says not asked.

## The man of about fifty-five who keeps a building four miles down a lane

**THE TANK IS STILL ABOUT A HAND'S BREADTH OVER ITS LIP, THE TWO CHALK LINES ARE STILL ON THE INSIDE OF THE BRICK, THE CRACK IS ABOUT THE LENGTH OF HIS HAND, THE NINE YEARS OF BEDS ARE STILL IN THE SECOND ROOM, AND NOBODY HAS ASKED FOR A FIFTH MEND IN FOUR MONTHS AND COUNTING.** He sent nothing to the yard on any of the ten days and nobody sent anything to him and he is named once and not visited. A crack in a tank is not a sixth protected thing.

## A man of about nineteen who counts

**HE DECLINED TO COUNT A POPULATION IN A YARD WITH A NUMBER NAILED TO A GATEPOST IN FRONT OF HIM, FOR THE SECOND TIME IN TWO MONTHS AND THE FIRST TIME ON A DAY WHEN THERE WAS A NUMBER IN FRONT OF HIM.** He counted nineteen people in a yard at ten, one nail, two buckets and three lines on a sheet, and said that was everything he could see and that looking for the rest would be the knock again only slower. He counted two things on a rise — the two ends of a building eleven miles off and the notch in the middle of its roof — and declined the rest. **He is nobody's clerk and is not to be turned into a counter of people, and he said out loud in a yard that he would rather be a man in no book with a number in him that he did not put there than be a man in a book with one.**

## The man of about thirty at a counter

**NOT IN THIS DISTRICT ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS, AND NO CHAPTER GAVE HIM A DAY.** He is at the sixteenth time down the eleven mile road and the next is the seventeenth, and the refusals about the ninth holding are seven and are a different figure from the eight, and a clerk entered on the eighth that this district has never once given that man a day to be here on.

## The man of about forty-one who keeps a road house at the top of the road

**HE IS NOT A KEEPER AND HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED TO BE ONE, AND NO KEEPER HAS EVER BEEN ON A PAGE AND THERE NEVER WAS ONE.** A clerk entered on the eighth of this month that he was standing outside his own door that morning, as he was on the fifteenth and the twentieth of last month, and was not asked a first thing, and that this district has never once given that man a day to be here on and gave him none this morning either.

## The man with the portfolio

**STILL NOT IN THIS DISTRICT, OFF THE PAGE, AND NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK SAYS WHERE HE IS.** The lease is unanswerable and he is a competitor and not an antagonist.

## Off the page in this block, and to be left there

**Cael Orin, whose name was said once out loud in a yard in Chapter 203 and was not said a second time in Chapters 204 to 260. Iven Tallow, the ninth design signature, who is not in this volume, does not appear, and is not to be named or foreshadowed by Batch 0002. Selik Marne, a wary ally and not a friend, who is not in this block. The nine keepers of the four generations, none of whom has ever been on a page and no one of whom is named. The Lock Council and the Quiet Registry, both of which are bodies that print and do not arrive, and neither of which acquired a face in this block. The four generations who arranged to know nothing about a frame in a yard. The Registrar-General, whose name is on a lease and on a portfolio nobody in this district has read. A man of about forty-four with a case, who is not yet on any page and is not yet anything else.**

# Character state after Volume 06 Batch 0002 (Chapters 261 to 270)

**Additive to every section above. Volume 06 is open. The figures below are the state at Chapter 270 and Batch 0003 inherits them.**

## The man the figure of twenty-one years is against

**HE OFFERED, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, THE SAFEST SHAPE A SECOND BOOK CAN HAVE, AND WAS REFUSED IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND THEN WAS TOLD THAT THERE IS NO ROOM IN THIS DISTRICT THAT NOBODY CAN WALK INTO.** He was not in the yard on the sixteenth of this month, which is the first of eleven days in the month that he has not stood in it, and nobody asked him why. He was in it on the seventeenth and said that a yard will hold anything you put in it if you say it out loud in it and that there is no column anywhere in this district that will hold him responsible for it. **On the twentieth he wrote twenty-four words on a slate and said he would come back for bread at seven, and neither of them said whether he would.** He is a Convener and the promotion is not a screen and the price is being paid. He is not a founder, not a holder, not a bearer of a toll, not a party of the fourth of four, not on the standing orders of the nine, and findable. **He was refused five things in a month by two women and every one of the five refusals was right, and he said in a lane on the thirteenth of this month that he is not going to stop offering, and a clerk entered that a man who has said he will go on offering has not acquired anything.**

## The registrar of this district's records office

**SHE REFUSED HIM IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE ON THE EIGHTEENTH OF THE SIXTH MONTH AND THEN SAID THE OTHER HALF OF WHAT SHE MEANT: THERE IS NO ROOM IN THIS DISTRICT THAT NOBODY CAN WALK INTO.** She was four feet off a trestle table for about nine minutes on the seventeenth while forty-one names were said in a yard and did not stop him, and said out loud that she wrote forty-one names on a page at about five in the morning on the sixth of this month and read nothing out and asked nobody and **that neither of them was asked.** On the sixteenth, in a doorway, she said *I know what is in it* and asked for three clauses to be put in the minute in her own hand, including that a person who has said *I know* and has not said the number has not refused anything and cannot be counted either way. She could not get her own door open at about half past five on the seventeenth and it took her about nine seconds, and a clerk entered that a hand that would not close on a pen and a hand that would not close on a door are the same hand and that the second time is worse. She stopped twice in four hundred yards on the eighteenth. **On the twentieth she said she wanted to have had nothing to do with what he is going to do on the twenty-first and asked for that to be written down as a thing she asked for and not as a thing he decided.** She is not a bearer, not a holder, not a steward and not a party of anything, and **a keeper of a room is not a thing she is and has never been asked to be.**

## The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing

**HE OPENED IT, AND HE SAID SO OUT LOUD IN A YARD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, AND NOBODY ARGUED WITH HIM.** He asked the question in about four feet of lane to about four people on the sixteenth and got nothing, and the same question in the yard on the seventeenth got forty-one names, and he said out loud that the difference between those two things is not the yard and not the hour and is him, standing up in front of more people, and that nobody is to tell him it was the broker. **He said in a yard on the fourteenth of this month that his left hand has not been right since the first of it and that he had said nothing for fourteen days, and the reason he gave for saying it now is that a man who asks the questions and cannot hold what he is asking about is a man somebody else can use.** He asked the woman of fifty-eight the same question five times; she did not answer four of them and answered the fifth. He could not get his fingers round a nail in a hasp on the first of this month, and worked it with both hands and could not shift it, and the nail did not move. He wrote twenty-four words on a slate for the fourteenth time. He was the one who read the broker's card out loud because the man with the case asked the yard to read it. He is not a founder and is not a keeper of anything.

## The man of about thirty-four who digs loam

**HE ENDED THE BLOCK ON THE FINDING THAT THE FIGURE HE PUT IN A YARD ON THE TENTH OF THE SIXTH MONTH WAS WRONG AND THE ARITHMETIC WAS RIGHT.** He counted forty-two lines in daylight on the eighteenth with the registrar holding the book, because his right hand could not turn a page corner, and said in a hundred and sixty-nine words: forty-two lines and forty names, and one of them is a person twice… I counted forty-one lines on the seventh at four in the morning with my own finger and I counted forty-two this morning with the same finger on the same book, and the arithmetic was right both times and the figure was wrong both times, and a man who gets a number right twice in eleven days by counting lines has been counting lines. He went down about two feet into the ditch behind the building with two doors on the eleventh and came up with a hand that does not close and said so out loud for the first time. He said the second printed sheet in this district is the best printing anybody has put up in this basin in his lifetime and that he has got no answer to it. He was not in the yard on the sixteenth and nobody sent for him. He is not one of the five and does not live in any of their rooms and nobody has asked him what a withdrawal would cost them.

## The man of fifty-six

**HE SAID OUT LOUD, IN FRONT OF ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE, THAT HE HAD BEEN PLEASED FOR SEVEN DAYS ABOUT A PRINTED RULE BEING BETTER THAN HIS OWN WHILE A MAN WITH A CASE SAT AT THE END OF A TABLE AND A MAN WROTE DOWN A LIST, AND THAT BEING PLEASED ABOUT IT IS THE REASON BOTH OF THOSE THINGS COULD HAPPEN IN THAT YARD.** He took a pen out of a man's fingers on the thirteenth and said nothing. He carried a pail off the man of about thirty-four who digs loam on the eighteenth and said nothing. He said the price of asking a question in front of about nineteen people on the seventeenth, once, and nobody in that yard was able to answer him. He said that four hundred and eleven is a count of people who wanted something when they answered it, and that he has been in this basin fifty-six years and has never once asked what a person was asked. He is right and says so with no pleasure in it. **THE DESCRIPTOR *man of fifty-six* FELL FROM THIRTY-SIX IN BATCH 0001 TO TWENTY-THREE IN THIS BLOCK, AND THE FRAME TABLE IN THE CANON CARD RECORDS THAT THE BLOCK GIVES HIM THE TWO MOST EXPENSIVE SENTENCES HE HAS HAD IN FIVE BLOCKS AND THAT HIS IMPORTANCE IS NOT MEASURABLE BY HOW OFTEN HIS DESCRIPTOR IS WRITTEN.**

## The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale

**SHE IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX AND SHE HAS STOOD AT THAT SCALE NINE YEARS AND WEIGHS ABOUT NINE SACKS A DAY, AND SHE IS THE PERSON WHO ASKED THE QUESTION THAT EXPOSED A BORROWED WORD AND SHE SUPPLIED NEITHER WORD: IN CHAPTER 205 SHE HEARD OPEN READ OUT FOUR TIMES IN A YARD AND ASKED WHAT WAS OPENING, AND A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX SAID OUT LOUD THAT THE DISTRICT HAD BEEN USING THE REGISTRY'S WORD FOR FOUR DAYS WITHOUT KNOWING IT AND THAT THE ASKING WAS DONE BY A WOMAN AT A WEIGHBRIDGE. **THE CLOSED TWO-WORDS CANON IS THAT *gates* IS THE REGISTRY'S WORD AND IS THE WORD ON THE STRANGER'S SCHEDULE, AND *locks* IS THE KEEPERS' WORD AND CAME FROM THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY AT A COUNTER IN CHAPTER 209, WHO SAID HIS HOUSE HAS CALLED THEM LOCKS SINCE BEFORE ANYBODY LIVING WAS BORN. THE EARLIER VERSION OF THIS LINE, WHICH SAID SHE GOT THE KEEPERS' WORD OUT OF A STRANGER'S SCHEDULE, WAS WRONG ON BOTH COUNTS AND IS WITHDRAWN.** KEEP THAT.** She was asked to be the keeper of a building on the eleventh of this month and said no in about four seconds. She was asked on the twelfth whether she would say more about the ninth of the eleven houses and said no with a reason, and gave the sentence the block turns on: **a thing said by a person who was not asked is the only thing in this district that is worth nothing, and if you ask me a question now then what I say next is an answer, and an answer is a thing with a person attached to it.** She disagreed with a printed rule out loud in a yard on the sixteenth, in the first of its three lines, and accepted the other two, and **took a risk doing it on a day when a sheet on a gatepost says that one copy of anything entered in that yard is to go to a body four hundred miles away, and nobody asked her to take it.** She said on the twentieth that her four empty houses are not going to be offered again before the first day of the season after this one. A bucket of hers is about eleven pounds full and **nobody counted the buckets on any of the twenty days of the two blocks and a clerk entered that nobody did.**

## The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households

**HE IS A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT AND NOT THIRTY-FOUR, and Volume 05's thirty-four is wrong and the closed canon of Chapters 146, 164, 166, 173, 179, 184, 187, 195 and 196 is a man of about forty-eight and he says himself in Chapter 184 that he is fifty-one.** **On the fifteenth of the sixth month a clerk asked him for his figure for the first time in five months and he refused, with a reason that is new and that a clerk entered as new: a figure of mine in a yard where a man with a case is sitting is a figure of mine in his list, and he does not have to ask me a second time, and I am not going to be a man who has to be asked twice.** His six stand at one signed, two refused and three not asked again and have not moved since the twenty-first of the fourth month: **the printed figures are fifty-one on the eleventh of this block, fifty-five on the fifteenth and sixty on the nineteenth, and a clerk has the figure without asking for it, and a figure a man will not repeat is not a refusal and is not a consent.** A man who keeps a count of six households is not a clerk of a house and is not one of the eight.

## The man of about thirty-four who keeps a road

Not the tally keeper and not the man who digs loam and not the man who mends fencing. He refused a second time on the twelfth. He walked his four miles on the fifteenth and told the nine houses on it about the man with a case and not one of the nine asked him for it. **On the nineteenth he came into the yard and said that one of the nine went and found the man with a case, and that he told him about it in about four seconds at the second gate and was not ashamed of it and was right not to be, and that he had stood in that yard on the fifteenth and told a man about a list without ever once asking him whether his house was on one.** He is not a bearer of the road and has never been asked to be one.

## The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat

**SHE IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR AND THIRTY-FOUR IS CLOSED CANON IN THIRTEEN USES ACROSS VOLUMES 04 AND 05, AT CHAPTERS 199, 200, 202, 203, 210, 211, 217, 219, 223, 226 TWICE, 234, 237 AND 242. A DESCRIPTOR IS NOT AVERAGED.** She is at the third house up the lane from the scale. **She said no to a third thing before she was asked on the fourteenth of this month in a printed span of nineteen words and would not say it twice: I HAVE SAID NO TO A MAN WITH A CASE AND HE IS NOT A MAN WITH A SLATE** She has kept a goat for eleven years and has held a bucket and a rope and a kid and nothing else heavier than about nine pounds since the spring. She is not a clerk of a house and not a keeper of a slate.

## The woman of about thirty-one who keeps a route for people who are not in any book

**SHE REFUSED A THIRD TIME IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS ON THE FOURTEENTH OF THE SIXTH MONTH AND GAVE THE REASON THE VOLUME IS ABOUT: if a man is hauling sacks for a man with a case then that man is a person who is not in a household by the ordinary meaning of those words, and if his name is on a list then a route is a thing somebody can buy, and I have spent nine years making sure there is nothing in this basin that can be bought that walks.** She came down four miles on the seventeenth because a man had come down her route in the morning and had not been one of hers, and said one thing that was not a refusal, including that two of the forty-one names said in that yard are women she has stood next to on a road in the dark and she did not know their names then and has not asked since. **SHE IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THIS BASIN WHO HAS EVER FOUND HIM WITHOUT BEING SENT, AND SHE IS HIS SISTER, AND THAT FACT IS ON NO PAGE IN THIS DISTRICT AND HAS NEVER BEEN ON ONE.** She is not a bearer of a route, has never been asked to be one, and is not a keeper.

## The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room with nothing over the door

**SHE WAS FOUND BY A MAN WITH A CASE BECAUSE A WOMAN AT A HOUSE ON THAT LANE TOLD HIM WHERE THE ROOM WAS, AND SHE HAS SAID OUT LOUD THAT EVERYTHING SHE SAID ON THE SEVENTH OF THE SIXTH MONTH WAS RIGHT AND NONE OF IT WAS ANY USE, and that the list he was holding was made before she knew he was on that lane, and that this was the first sum of four years of arithmetic that came out wrong.** She refused him in about four seconds in her own room and told him he could have the bench, and said that the bench is not a copy and is not a list and that eleven notches are eleven and are not a figure about anybody. She is not a clerk of a house and not a bearer of a bench.

## The man of about forty-four with a case

**NEW TO THE PROSE, and the one planned antagonist this block spends, and HE IS A METHOD AND NOT A FACE.** He came into the yard of Lot Seventeen at about ten in the morning on the thirteenth of the sixth month, said in the first minute that he brokers names into books for money and that he has never been in a place where a person said no to him, and asked the yard to read his own three-line card. He was given the end of the trestle table. He bought a list of the people in this district who are not in a household out of a man of about twenty-three for eleven coppers a week. **He lost it in about nine minutes on the seventeenth, when forty-one names were said out loud in a yard a stranger can walk into, and said one uncounted sentence: That is not a list. I have been doing this four years and I could not sell that in any of them, and I have been sitting at this table since the thirteenth waiting for something I could sell.** He left a slate on the end of the table with nothing on it and went out of that gate at about two in the afternoon on the nineteenth. **A STAY COUNTED WITHOUT THE DAY OF ARRIVAL IS FIVE DAYS, BEING THE FOURTEENTH TO THE EIGHTEENTH, AND HE IS NOT IN THIS DISTRICT AND NOTHING IN THE BLOCK SAYS WHERE HE WENT.** He is a man who is not a clerk of a house, is not a bearer of anything, and is not one of the eight.

## The man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart

He brought nine sacks down to the scale on the morning of the fourteenth of this month, having hauled for the man with a case since the thirteenth without being asked, and on the nineteenth he sent nine more down to the scale and did not take any money for them. **He is not the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room with nothing over the door and not the man of about nineteen who counts, and a clerk entered that the three of them are three people in three trades inside two miles of each other in this district and that the trade is the identifier and never carries the identification alone.** He is on the list and nobody in this district has seen the list and nobody has counted it.

## The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing

**HE SAID YES TO ELEVEN COPPERS A WEEK IN ABOUT ONE SECOND ON THE THIRTEENTH OF THE SIXTH MONTH, HAVING BEEN REFUSED IT EIGHT TIMES IN FIVE MONTHS, AND GAVE THE REASON: a man with a case has not spent five months asking me to do it. He has not made me a refusal first. I have been a refusal in this district for five months and I have got very good at it and it has never once cost me anything, and I would like to be paid for four months of being a refusal.** He wrote a list in about an hour, his right hand cramped, and a man of fifty-six took the pen out of his fingers. He said in a yard on the sixteenth that he had been in the room where the books are on the afternoon of the seventh of this month and that the second book was open on the shelf and that he did not read it and has not stopped seeing it, and that he was saying it in that yard before anybody said it for him. **HE STOOD UP ON THE END OF THE TRESTLE TABLE ON THE SEVENTEENTH AND SAID FORTY-ONE NAMES OUT LOUD IN ABOUT NINE MINUTES IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, AND A MAN OF ABOUT NINETEEN COUNTED THEM. He could not hold a pen on the eighteenth. The arrangement was finished on the eighteenth in about four seconds. HE SAID THE WHOLE OF IT OUT LOUD IN A YARD ON THE TWENTIETH AND THEN SAT DOWN, AND HE IS NOT PAID AND HIS HAND DOES NOT CLOSE.** A clerk entered that a man who says yes to a thing he was refused by this district has not refused this district, and that a refusal a man makes of somebody outside this district is not a refusal this district made, and that the eight did not move.

## A man of about nineteen who counts

**HE COUNTED THE FORTY-ONE NAMES THAT WERE SAID OUT LOUD IN THE YARD OF LOT SEVENTEEN ON THE SEVENTEENTH OF THE SIXTH MONTH, and a clerk entered that he counted them and that nobody asked him to, and that a man who counts a thing when there is a number in front of him is not a counter of people and is nobody's clerk.** He counted the people in that yard at ten on the sixteenth and got six and said out loud that a smaller number in a yard is not a smaller yard and is a figure about six people and not about a yard. He counted nine mats through an open door on the eleventh and stopped, and said that a mat is not a figure about anybody. He counted eleven coppers onto a trestle table on the fifteenth. He is nobody's clerk and is not to be turned into a counter of people.

## The woman of fifty-eight

**SHE CARRIES WATER TO ELEVEN HOUSES AND HAS FOR NINETEEN YEARS AND NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT HAD EVER ASKED HER ANYTHING UNTIL THE FOURTH OF THE SIXTH MONTH, and she comes down that bank with two buckets at about six most mornings and has done for nineteen years, and a clerk entered that nobody has ever asked her and that the record about the nineteen years says not asked.** She did not answer the question about the ninth house on the fourth of the sixth month, and on the twelfth, and on the sixteenth, and on the eighteenth. **ON THE NINETEENTH SHE PUT A BUCKET DOWN AND SAT ON A STEP OUTSIDE THE SECOND HOUSE UP THAT LANE, WHICH NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT HAD EVER SEEN HER SIT ON, and said thirteen words — I SAID IT BECAUSE YOU ASKED ME FIVE TIMES AND I AM FIFTY-EIGHT — and then said the rest: the woman in the ninth of those houses is not one of the four hundred and eleven, and the woman asked for a second bucket in the second week of last month, and a person who asks for a second bucket is a person who has answered a question, and that is what the four hundred and eleven is made of, and it is not a lie and it is not a mistake, and I would not want her on a list because of a bucket.** She did not say anything back to the man of fifty-six and was not asked to. She is not a clerk of a house. A bucket of hers is about eleven pounds full and **nobody counted the buckets on any of the ten days of this block and a clerk entered that nobody did.**

## The man of about thirty at a counter

**NOT IN THIS DISTRICT ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS OF THIS BLOCK, AND NO CHAPTER GAVE HIM A DAY.** He is at the sixteenth time down the eleven mile road and the next is the seventeenth, and the refusals about the ninth holding are seven and are a different figure from the eight.

## The man of about forty-one who keeps a road house at the top of the road

**HE IS NOT A KEEPER AND HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED TO BE ONE, AND NO KEEPER HAS EVER BEEN ON A PAGE AND THERE NEVER WAS ONE.** He has no day in this district, this district has never once given him one, and neither block gave him one.

## The man with the portfolio

**STILL NOT IN THIS DISTRICT, OFF THE PAGE, AND NOTHING IN EITHER BLOCK SAYS WHERE HE IS.**

## Off the page in this block, and to be left there

**Cael Orin, whose name was said once out loud in a yard in Chapter 203 and was not said a second time in Chapters 204 to 270. Iven Tallow, the ninth design signature, who is not in this volume, does not appear, and is not to be named or foreshadowed by Batch 0003. Selik Marne, a wary ally and not a friend, who is not in this block. The nine keepers of the four generations, none of whom has ever been on a page and no one of whom is named. The Lock Council and the Quiet Registry, both of which are bodies that print and do not arrive, and neither of which acquired a face in this block. The four generations who arranged to know nothing about a frame in a yard. The Registrar-General, whose name is on a lease and on a portfolio nobody in this district has read.**

# Character state after Volume 06 Batch 0003 (Chapters 271 to 280)

**Additive to every section above. Volume 06 is open. The figures below are the state at Chapter 280 and Batch 0004 inherits them.**

## The man the figure of twenty-one years is against

**HE ASKED A MAN TO STAND IN A DOORWAY IN A YARD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, WAS REFUSED IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, ASKED THE SAME QUESTION AGAIN IN THE SAME WORDS, AND THE MAN SAID YES AND NAMED A PRICE. HE PAID IT, IN TWO HUNDRED AND FIFTY-SIX WORDS, IN A YARD A STRANGER CAN WALK INTO, SAYING THAT HE WAS GOING TO RING A BELL FOUR MILES OUT PAST THE LOAMS AND THAT A BELL TAKES THE NAMES OF EVERYBODY WHO HEARS IT, AND THAT HE WAS NOT GOING TO BE THE ONE HOLDING THE ROPE. HE CHOSE THE PARTIAL LOT AND SAID THE REASON OUT LOUD IN A ROOM WITH A SHUT DOOR IN IT, IN THREE HUNDRED AND NINETY-TWO WORDS, BEFORE ANYTHING WAS SIGNED, AND THE REASON IS A CHILD OF ABOUT EIGHT WHO HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED ANYTHING BY ANYBODY IN THIS DISTRICT.** He is a Convener and the promotion is not a screen and the price is being paid. He is not a founder, not a holder, not a bearer of a toll, not a party of anything, and findable. **HE WAS REFUSED SEVEN THINGS IN TWENTY DAYS BY TWO WOMEN AND WAS GRANTED ONE OF THEM, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT HE ACQUIRED NOTHING BY ANY OF THE SEVEN. ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH HE SAID THE LESSON THIS VOLUME HAS BEEN TEACHING OUT LOUD, IN A ROOM, IN THREE HUNDRED AND NINETY-TWO WORDS, AND ACTED ON IT THE SAME AFTERNOON, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A MAN WHO CONVENES NINE SETTLEMENTS IS NOT A REPRESENTATIVE OF A SETTLEMENT THAT HAS NOT ASKED TO BE REPRESENTED.**

## The registrar of this district's records office

**ON THE TWENTY-SECOND OF THIS MONTH SHE GRANTED A THING HE HAD NOT ASKED FOR, IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, IN ABOUT NINE FEET OF LANE OUTSIDE THE OFFICE: that the reason the lot is not a complete lot be printed on the face of the piece of paper in her own hand and not in a clerk's. AND THEN SHE SAID THE THING HE HAD NOT ASKED FOR: I have never once in nine years put a sentence of my own in that book, and I said on the twentieth of this month that I wanted to have had nothing to do with what you are going to do, and what I am going to do now is the opposite of that and I am doing it anyway and I would like it written down that I said on the twentieth that I wanted nothing to do with it and that on the twenty-second of this month I have put my own hand on a piece of paper about a bell.** **SHE WROTE THE CHARTER IN HER OWN HAND AND DID NOT WRITE THE LOT AT ALL: the lot is in three lines in the hand of the man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing and a fourth line in the hand of the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing, and her hand stopped in the middle of the word *person* in the third line of the charter in the middle of the fourth attempt and would not finish it, and she told the man of about twenty-three to rub it out and write it again, and he did.** On the twenty-eighth she read the lot once herself in a lane and said that the third of its four lines is the one she has not been able to put down. **ON THE TWENTY-NINTH SHE PUT THE LOT INTO THE FIRST OF THE TWO BOOKS ON THAT SHELF AT ABOUT FIVE IN THE AFTERNOON AND THE CHARTER WENT IN UNDER IT, AND SAID OUT LOUD TO ABOUT NINE PEOPLE THAT SHE HAD SAID IN A LANE THE EVENING BEFORE THAT SHE WAS NOT GOING TO PUT IT IN THAT NIGHT, AND THAT A NIGHT IS A NIGHT AND AN AFTERNOON IS AN AFTERNOON.** **ON THE LAST EVENING, IN THE DOORWAY OF THAT ROOM, SHE SAID THAT SHE TOLD HIM ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH THAT SHE WAS NOT GOING TO PUT HER OWN HAND ON EITHER OF HIS TWO SHAPES, AND THAT SHE HAS WRITTEN ONE OF THEM OUT IN HER OWN HAND FROM THE FIRST WORD TO THE LAST AND PUT HER OWN HAND UNDER THE OTHER AT ABOUT FIVE THAT AFTERNOON, AND THAT A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR READ IT OUT, AND THAT SHE DID NOT TAKE HER HAND BACK OFF IT, AND THAT THERE IS NO ROOM IN THIS DISTRICT THAT NOBODY CAN WALK INTO AND SHE HAS NOT STOPPED SAYING THAT, AND THAT SHE DOES NOT KNOW WHAT TO CALL WHAT SHE HAS DONE AND IS NOT GOING TO CALL IT ANYTHING TONIGHT.** She is not a bearer, not a holder, not a steward and not a party of anything, and a keeper of a room is not a thing she is and has never been asked to be.

## The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing

**HE WENT UP A FRAME ABOUT ELEVEN FEET HIGH FOUR MILES OUT PAST THE LOAMS ON THE TWENTY-THIRD OF THIS MONTH WITH HIS RIGHT HAND, BECAUSE HIS LEFT HAND HAS NOT BEEN RIGHT SINCE THE FIRST OF IT, AND HIS LEFT HAND HAD NOTHING TO HOLD, AND HE CAME DOWN THE SIDE OF A HEADSTOCK OF IRON AND TOOK THE SKIN OFF THE INSIDE OF HIS LEFT FOREARM FROM THE WRIST TO THE ELBOW IN ONE STRIP, AND SAID SO OUT LOUD IN THAT YARD ELEVEN MINUTES LATER.** He tied nine inches of rope to two feet of new rope in the afternoon of the twenty-fourth and **PULLED THE BELL AT ABOUT TWO IN THE MORNING THAT NIGHT.** He wrote the fourth line of the lot because the man of about twenty-three could not hold a pen, and he read both documents out, and he read the sheet of nine names out. He argued in a yard on the twenty-sixth for the accurate lot, on the ground that a lot about the room is the same shape as the third column of the covenant, and lost, **and on the twenty-eighth a man of fifty-six said in a yard that the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing had said a thing about that shape in a room on the twenty-seventh and that he had been in that room when it was said, which is the argument credited to the man who made it,** and said in a room on the twenty-seventh that he has not changed his mind and would have written it and would have been right about the hole and wrong about the child. **HE ENDED THE BLOCK IN A LANE BEHIND A BANK AT ABOUT HALF PAST SEVEN ON THE LAST NIGHT IN TWO HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-NINE WORDS, AND THE LAST OF THEM IS: I am not going to be the man who takes it back out of that book.** He is not a founder and is not a keeper of anything and has never been asked to be.

## The man of about thirty-four who digs loam

**HE SAID NO IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS ON THE TWENTY-FIRST OF THIS MONTH, IN A HUNDRED AND THIRTY-TWO WORDS, ON THE GROUND THAT A MAN WHO CANNOT HOLD A PEN SHUT CANNOT HOLD A SHEET FLAT, AND HE WAS ASKED THE SAME QUESTION AGAIN IN THE SAME WORDS AND SAID YES AND SAID WHAT IT COST IN THREE HUNDRED AND NINETY-TWO WORDS, AND THE COST WAS THAT THE MAN WHO SENT HIM WOULD HAVE TO SAY IT OUT LOUD IN A YARD IN DAYLIGHT FIRST.** He read the two walls out loud under the frame and said in fifteen words that either the rule is not a rule or one of those two hands is wrong, **and a clerk entered that this district has not worked out which and is not going to work it out on that morning.** He stood in the doorway of the building with two doors from about half past one in the morning on the night of the twenty-fourth until the bell stopped, and **took a sheet of paper off a table in four goes and the second go put it on the ground at his feet, and he picked it up off the ground with two fingers and folded it in four the way it had been folded and put it inside his coat, and he said so out loud in that lane himself and nobody asked him about it.** He said in a yard on the twenty-eighth that a table with four legs and a man in a doorway taking a piece of paper off it is a counter, and that he has not got an argument against that and has not got an argument for it either. He said in a room on the twenty-ninth that a charter that says a person was not asked is covered by it is wrong. He is not one of the five and does not live in any of their rooms and nobody has asked him what a withdrawal would cost them.

## The man of fifty-six

**HE WALKED FOUR MILES OUT PAST THE LOAMS IN THE DARK ON THE NIGHT OF THE TWENTY-FOURTH TO BE STANDING UNDER THAT FRAME WHEN THE BELL WENT, GOT THERE ABOUT TWO MINUTES AFTER IT STOPPED, AND WALKED EIGHT MILES IN THE DARK, AND SAID IT IN A ROOM WITH A SHUT DOOR ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH IN A HUNDRED AND SIXTY-NINE WORDS, INCLUDING THAT HE GOT THERE ABOUT TWO MINUTES AFTER IT STOPPED AND FOUND A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR STANDING UNDER THAT FRAME WITH A ROPE IN HIS HAND ABOUT THREE FEET LONG, INCLUDING THAT HE WAS NOT AFRAID OF TONIGHT AND WAS AFRAID OF THE MORNING AFTER AND COULD NOT WRITE DOWN WHAT HE WAS AFRAID OF.** He said in a room on the twenty-ninth that four hundred and eleven is a count of people who answered a door, that forty-one is a count of lines in one book in this district, that the difference is three hundred and seventy and is arithmetic and is not a finding, and that **a claim about the whole of this district is made out of a count of the people who opened a door, and they have rung a bell, and a bell does not knock.** **AND ON THE LAST DAY OF THE BLOCK HE SAID IN A YARD THAT HE HAS BEEN WRONG ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN FOR SIX WEEKS, THAT A WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT SAT DOWN ON A STEP OUTSIDE THE SECOND HOUSE UP THAT LANE ON THE NINETEENTH OF THIS MONTH AND TOLD HIM, AND THAT HE SAID SO OUT LOUD IN THAT LANE THE SAME EVENING AND NOBODY ARGUED WITH HIM ABOUT IT AND NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM ABOUT IT SINCE — WHICH IS CHAPTER 269 AND NOT A NEW SCENE, AND A MAN WHO HAS BEEN WRONG FOR SIX WEEPS IS NOT A MAN WHO INVENTS A YARD TO SAY SO IN.** He said in a yard on the last day of the block that a yard is the worst possible place to find out that a document you are about to publish is wrong, and that he was going to let a cart driver's sentence stand and add nothing to it. **THE DESCRIPTOR *man of fifty-six* ROSE FROM TWENTY-TWO IN BATCH 0002 TO THIRTY-TWO IN THIS BLOCK, AND THE FRAME TABLE RECORDS THAT THE BLOCK GIVES HIM THE HOUR THE BELL STARTED AND THE MOMENT IT STOPPED, AND THAT A MAN'S IMPORTANCE TO A BLOCK IS NOT MEASURABLE BY HOW OFTEN HIS DESCRIPTOR IS WRITTEN.**

## The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale

**SHE IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX AND SHE HAS STOOD AT THAT SCALE NINE YEARS AND WEIGHS ABOUT NINE SACKS A DAY, AND SHE IS THE PERSON WHO ASKED THE QUESTION THAT EXPOSED A BORROWED WORD AND SHE SUPPLIED NEITHER WORD. THE CLOSED TWO-WORDS CANON IS UNCHANGED AND IS TO BE KEPT EXACTLY: *gates* IS THE REGISTRY'S WORD AND IS THE WORD ON THE STRANGER'S SCHEDULE, AND *locks* IS THE KEEPERS' WORD AND CAME FROM THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY AT A COUNTER IN CHAPTER 209.** She said the word *stool* out loud in a yard on the twenty-second of this month before anybody asked her, and then disagreed out loud with the chair at the end of the trestle table, and was in her own house at about two in the morning on the night of the twenty-fourth with the door shut and a blanket over her shoulders and did not come out of it while that bell was going, and came out of her own door about half a minute after it stopped and said out loud that she has stood at that scale for nine years and has never once heard it. **She said on the twenty-sixth, in a hundred and eighty words, that both the men in that yard were talking about a child and neither of them had been in the room, and that she wants to know what happens to him in ten years, and that there is nobody in this district who could tell her, including the man asking the question.** Her four empty houses are not offered again before the first day of the season after this one. A bucket of hers is about eleven pounds full and **nobody counted the buckets on any of the ten days of this block.**

## The man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally for six households

**HE IS A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT AND NOT THIRTY-FOUR, and he says himself in Chapter 184 that he is fifty-one. HE HELD A PAGE OPEN FOR ABOUT AN HOUR ON THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF THIS MONTH BECAUSE A MAN WITH A BAD HAND ASKED HIM TO, AND AFTERWARDS STOOD AT THE TOP OF THE BANK AND COULD NOT BRING A FIGURE INTO FOCUS AT THE LENGTH OF HIS OWN ARM, AND SAID SEVENTY-FOUR WORDS INCLUDING I AM NOT GOING TO BLIND MYSELF OVER A PIECE OF PAPER, and then MY SIX HAVE NOT MOVED ON ANY OF THE SIXTY-SIX DAYS AND I AM NOT GOING TO BE A MAN WHO READS A FIGURE OUT OF A LOT ABOUT A NIGHT.** His six stand at one signed, two refused and three not asked again and have not moved since the twenty-first of the fourth month: **the printed figures are sixty-one on the twenty-first of this month, sixty-three on the twenty-third, sixty-six on the twenty-sixth and seventy on the thirtieth, and a clerk entered them out of the ladder and did not ask him on any of the ten days, and he said the figure on none of them. A figure a man will not repeat is not a refusal and is not a consent.** A man who keeps a count of six households is not a clerk of a house and is not one of the eight.

## The man of about thirty-four who keeps a road

**He was the last person through the front door of the building with two doors on the night of the twenty-fourth, at about nine minutes after the bell started, having been at the top of the bank. He walked his four miles in the morning of the twenty-sixth and said, in a hundred and ninety-two words, that a quarter of an hour is about as long as it takes a person to get a coat on and get a child up and get out of a door, that a sheet of paper cannot do that, and that anything this district is going to write down is going to be read by somebody who is not in it and who will not be a quarter of an hour away from it. On the twenty-third he said, in a hundred and forty-four words, that about nine people walked past his gate going to the bell and about nine walked back and both lots of nine were the same nine, and that there is not one other thing in this district where that is true.** He is not a bearer of the road and has never been asked to be one.

## The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat

**SHE IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR AND THIRTY-FOUR IS CLOSED CANON IN THIRTEEN USES ACROSS VOLUMES 04 AND 05. A DESCRIPTOR IS NOT AVERAGED.** She is at the third house up the lane from the scale. **She wrote her own name on the piece of paper on the table outside the building with two doors on the night of the twenty-fourth, in her own hand, in the dark, and then stood about four feet back and watched the other eight do it and did not say anything to anybody about what she was doing.** On the twenty-third of this month she said one uncounted thing at the foot of her own gate about a man with an arm out of his sleeve and said she was not going to say it twice. She is not a clerk of a house and not a keeper of a slate and not a bearer of a goat in anybody's sentence.

## The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room with nothing over the door

**THE BLOCK'S CENTRAL NEW SPEAKER, AND THE PERSON WHO BROUGHT THE CHILD INTO THE PROSE.** The room is about nine feet by fourteen, there is nothing over the door, and there is a bench about nine feet long with about eleven notches cut in the underside of the seat at intervals that are not even, and about nine people sleep there most nights. **On the twenty-fifth of this month she said out loud in a yard, in sixteen words, that there is a child in that room and he heard the bell and he did not come, and then said the rest of it, in two hundred and ten words, and she is not going to say what he is called.** On the twenty-seventh she was asked one question in a room with a shut door in it and answered it, and refused with a reason in a hundred and sixty-three words: a description of where a child of about eight sleeps in this district is two miles long and about nine houses and one lane with nothing over the door on it, and any of you could read it and know where he is, and a man with a case found that room in nine minutes on the fourteenth of this month because a woman at a house on that lane told him where it was, and that was not her fault and it was the end of the four years. On the twenty-ninth she was at the end of that lane about two miles short of her room and said one uncounted thing: there are five lines in that book and I can only read three of them, and I am going back up that two miles tonight and I am not going to knock on a door about it. She is not a clerk of a house and not a bearer of a bench and not a keeper of anything.

## A child of about eight

**NEW TO THE PROSE IN TWO HUNDRED AND SEVENTY CHAPTERS, AND DESCRIBED AND NEVER NAMED.** He sleeps in the room with nothing over the door, on a bench with about eleven notches cut in the underside of the seat. **He was awake at about half past three in the morning on the night of the twenty-fourth and heard the bell from about two miles off and did not come to the building, because nobody told him there was a bell, and because the woman who keeps that room is not a person who is sent for and did not go back down that two miles in the dark to fetch him.** He is about eight years old, he is in no book, he is not on the piece of paper and not in the lot and not in the charter, and **he has never been asked anything by anybody in this district in his life, and a clerk entered that twice on purpose and said that the two sentences are the same sentence.** He is the reason this district chose a partial lot, and he is the cost of it, and he is spent in this block and is not to be used again. **HE IS NOT THE BOY OF NINE WHOSE NAME IS IN A COLUMN IN A BUILDING TWO DAYS OF ROAD AWAY, AND THE TWO ARE NOT THE SAME PERSON AND ARE NOT ADDED TOGETHER.** He is not a clerk of a house, not a bearer, not a party of anything, and not a figure about anybody.

## The man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing

**HE WROTE THREE OF THE FOUR LINES OF THE LOT LONGHAND ON THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF THIS MONTH AND COULD NOT HOLD A PEN FOR THE FOURTH, AND THE FOURTH IS IN ANOTHER MAN'S HAND. HIS VOICE WENT AT ABOUT THE SIXTH OF THE NINE NAMES HE READ OUT ON THE TWENTY-FIFTH AND HE COULD NOT SPEAK ABOVE A WHISPER FOR THE REST OF THAT DAY AND FOR THE DAY AFTER, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A VOICE IS NOT A FIGURE ABOUT A MAN AND IS NOT A BEARER AND IS NOT A PRICE OF ANYTHING. On the twenty-seventh he wrote the word *person* in the third line of the charter in the registrar's room, and she told him to rub it out and write it again and he did.** He still has not been paid and his hand does not close. **On the twenty-fifth he said in a whisper, in a hundred and thirty-four words, that he is not going to write that child's name down anywhere and is not going to ask him whether he may.** He is not a clerk of a house and is not one of the eight.

## A man of about nineteen who counts

**Counted the four legs of a table outside a building with two doors in Chapter 272 and said out loud that four legs is not a figure about anybody. Stood in a lane from about two in the morning on the night of the twenty-fourth, counted the people who came through a front door, WOULD NOT GIVE THE NUMBER TO A CLERK, and said out loud that a number a man counts in the dark at the end of a lane is a figure about a man counting and not about the people.** He is nobody's clerk and is not a counter of people.

## The woman of fifty-eight

**SHE READ A FIVE-LINE CHARTER BACK IN A ROOM ON THE TWENTY-NINTH OF THIS MONTH, HAVING BEEN ASKED AND SAID YES IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND SHE READ ALL FIVE LINES, AND SHE STOPPED AT THE FIFTH FOR ABOUT NINE SECONDS AND THEN READ IT OUT LOUD ANYWAY AND SAID WHY SHE HAD STOPPED, IN TWO HUNDRED AND SEVENTY-EIGHT WORDS: one of the eleven houses she carries water to is a house in the ground in the shadow of the Hall, the woman in it is not one of the four hundred and eleven and is not on any sheet, and she asked for a second bucket in the second week of last month.** **ON THE TWENTY-EIGHTH SHE SAID OUT LOUD IN A LANE THAT SHE DOES NOT KNOW WHETHER SHE WENT INTO THAT BUILDING ON THE NIGHT OF THE TWENTY-FOURTH, AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO BE ASKED, AND THAT ANYBODY WHO WORKS THAT OUT AND WRITES IT DOWN IS GOING TO BE WRITING DOWN A THING ABOUT A WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT AND NOT ABOUT A NIGHT.** She is not a clerk of a house and her two buckets were not counted on any of the ten days. **NINETEEN YEARS AND ONE QUESTION ASKED AND ANSWERED ON THE FIFTH ASKING, AND THE ANSWER IS ON THE RECORD AND NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT HAS ACTED ON IT.**

## The man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart

**HE WALKED INTO THE ROOM WHERE THE BOOKS ARE ON THE MORNING OF THE THIRTIETH OF THIS MONTH, HAVING READ TWO PIECES OF PAPER IN A BOOK IN THERE THAT NOBODY HAD ASKED HIM TO READ AND THAT NOBODY STOPPED HIM FROM READING, AND HE CAME OUT AND SAID IN A YARD, IN TWO HUNDRED AND FORTY-TWO WORDS, WHAT THE TWO OF THEM SAY WHEN YOU PUT THEM SIDE BY SIDE, WHICH IS THAT THE SECOND OF THEM IS NOT TRUE, AND HE ENDED BY ASKING THAT IT BE ENTERED THAT THE MAN WHO SAID IT IS A MAN WHO DRIVES A CART AND THAT HE IS NOT ONE OF THE THINGS HE IS TALKING ABOUT.** **HE IS NOT THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A ROOM WITH NOTHING OVER THE DOOR AND NOT THE MAN OF ABOUT NINETEEN WHO COUNTS, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT THE THREE OF THEM ARE THREE PEOPLE IN THREE TRADES INSIDE TWO MILES OF EACH OTHER AND THE TRADE IS THE IDENTIFIER AND NEVER CARRIES THE IDENTIFICATION ALONE.** He hauled nine sacks down to the scale on the morning of the fourteenth of the month before this one for a man with a case and took no money for them, and he is on the list that man took out of this district and nobody in this district has seen the list and nobody has counted it.

## The woman of about thirty-one who keeps a route for people who are not in any book

**SHE IS NOT IN THIS DISTRICT ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS OF THIS BLOCK AND IS IN IT IN ONE CLERK'S ENTRY ONLY, IN CHAPTER 273, IN THE SENTENCE THAT SAYS THE NINE INCHES OF ROPE WENT INTO THE HANDS OF A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING AND THAT SHE WAS NOT IN THAT YARD AND HAD NOT BEEN THERE AND DOES NOT COME TO THAT FRAME. HER EXIT CLAUSE IS NOT SPENT AND HER THREE REFUSALS WERE NOT EXTENDED AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS BLOCK ASKED HER ANYTHING.** She is not a bearer of a route, has never been asked to be one, and is not a keeper.

## The man of about thirty at a counter

**NOT IN THIS DISTRICT ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS OF THIS BLOCK, AND NO CHAPTER GAVE HIM A DAY.** He is at the sixteenth time down the eleven mile road and the next is the seventeenth, and the refusals about the ninth holding are seven and are a different figure from the eight.

## The man of about forty-one who keeps a road house at the top of the road

**HE IS NOT A KEEPER AND HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED TO BE ONE, AND NO KEEPER HAS EVER BEEN ON A PAGE AND THERE NEVER WAS ONE.** He has no day in this district, this district has never once given him one, and this block gave him none.

## The man with the portfolio

**STILL NOT IN THIS DISTRICT, OFF THE PAGE, AND NOTHING IN THIS BLOCK SAYS WHERE HE IS.**

## Off the page in this block, and to be left there

**Cael Orin, whose name was said once out loud in a yard in Chapter 203 and was not said a second time in Chapters 204 to 280. Iven Tallow, the ninth design signature, who is not in this volume, does not appear, and is not to be named or foreshadowed by Batch 0004. Selik Marne, a wary ally and not a friend, who is not in this block. The nine keepers of the four generations, none of whom has ever been on a page and no one of whom is named. The Lock Council and the Quiet Registry, both of which are bodies that print and do not arrive, and neither of which acquired a face in this block. The four generations who arranged to know nothing about a frame in a yard. The Registrar-General, whose name is on a lease and on a portfolio nobody in this district has read. A man of about forty-four with a case, who went out of that gate on the nineteenth of the sixth month and whose list nobody in this district has seen and nobody has counted.**
