She says so out loud, she says she is not going to stand in a yard of nineteen people and say that she did not, and she gives her reason, which is that two men have asked her this month whether her hearing is going and she has worked out that neither of the two of them was asking about her hearing.** On the twenty-eighth she said one thing out loud to the boy of about nineteen and not to the table: that she heard every part of a name said in that yard the day before, that she wrote one line and put nothing under it, that a person who has heard a thing and written nothing of it is in a different position from a person who has heard nothing at all, and that she is not going to say which of those two she is, and that the boy could turn his page over whenever he liked and it would not make her say it out loud. **SHE IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THIS DISTRICT HOLDING A CLEAN COPY OF A NAME, SHE HAS PUT IT ON NOTHING, NOBODY HAS ASKED HER FOR IT, AND SHE IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT AND HAS NOT BEEN MADE ONE, WHICH IS EXACTLY WHAT THE MENDER SAID AT Chapter 426 AND EXACTLY WHAT SHE HAS NOW SAID IN HER OWN WORDS TWENTY-FOUR HOURS LATER.** She entered three things on the twenty-seventh: that a name was said, that a boy put a page face down, and that a man said no to a request in about four seconds and gave a reason. She entered no name. She has not been asked for a name by anybody in that yard since the nineteenth of the tenth month.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING. TWO THINGS FIXED AROUND HIM AND NOTHING CHANGED IN HIM.** His cloth stayed on all ten days and he did not read a figure off a wall in this block. The review found that the hand put on the boards at Chapter 426 was at the near end of that table while the whole stopping beat, and *Stop there*, and *you are not a post*, and *I am not going to be the man who finishes a sentence that starts a job* were his, and were at the far end. **The hand is now his and is at the far end, and the near-end man no longer exists in that chapter.** The review also found that at Chapter 429 he answered a question the boy of about nineteen had asked the clerk, and **the prose now says that he answered it and she did not, and that two people at that table said afterwards that they had not known until that moment that a person could be asked a question in that yard and have it taken off them.** He counted a boy of about nineteen at Chapter 428, which is still the first time a man with a cloth on his hand has counted anybody in that district. At Chapter 430 the three-question count that hung on the wrong man is now anchored to the ditch, and **he says out loud that the three went to two men and the two men are not the same one, and that he would like the yard to notice that before it decides the third one counts.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP. NOTHING CHANGED IN HIM AND TWO FIGURES AROUND HIM CORRECTED.** He carried the thing in his mouth since the sixth of this month counted exclusive of the start day, which is twenty-one days on the twenty-seventh and **twenty-two on the twenty-eighth, and the review found the twenty-eighth printing twenty-one in both places it stands and both are now twenty-two.** His line about a fifth time the nine words were said out loud pointed at the twenty-fifth, and **the nine words were said on the twenty-fourth, and Chapter 427 says they were not said on the twenty-fifth, and it now reads three days ago.** The tent is still rolled on the back of the hand-cart. He was given nothing on all ten days and nobody asked him about it. He is the only man in that yard who could have stopped the name going into a book and is not going to be able to stop it going into anybody's head, and he still does not know what he is going to do on the last day of the month.
- **THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN. ONE THING FIXED AND THE POSITION HE HOLDS IS NOW SAID OUT LOUD INSTEAD OF ONLY BEING SHOWN.** At Chapter 430 the sentence that credited him with counting forty words sat one line under the protagonist's one-word *No.*, and so read as a count of the *No.*, and he was made to count words that did not exist yet immediately after the block's tightest exchange. **He now has the count ready before the sentence is said, which is the way he has done it all month, and the count came to forty, and he read the number back to himself.** He put his page face down for the fourth time in this district and the first of the four about somebody else's page. He was not told what was going to be said and he said he would rather be standing in that yard not knowing what a thing is than be told. **HE WAS TOLD NOTHING, HE ASKED WHETHER HE MAY PUT A NAME DOWN, HE WAS TOLD IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS THAT HE MAY NOT, AND HE PUT HIS PAGE DOWN ANYWAY, AND THE CLERK TOLD HIM ON THE TWENTY-EIGHTH THAT HE COULD TURN IT OVER WHENEVER HE LIKED AND THAT IT WOULD NOT MAKE HER SAY IT OUT LOUD.**
- **THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST. NOTHING CHANGED.** He said nothing out loud for six days and then said, in forty words a man of about nineteen counted, that nobody had asked him for a name and nobody was going to and that he was not saying it because of anything said the day before. He has never been called anything at all by anybody in that yard. He has a right arm in a coat and it did not move. He is not the author of the name and said so.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM.** Nothing changed. He was in that ditch on all ten days, his right arm did not go above the level of that shoulder on any of them, and nobody said one word to him about it on any of them. On the twenty-eighth he worked out in the water that the four of them had spent a morning working out whose name it was and that he is not going to help, and that if it is his he will say so when somebody asks him and if it is not he is not going to say it is not, **because the second one is the same kind of talking as the first one.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD.** Nothing changed. He came up that lane on all ten days, got the four figures off that wall as he went past on all ten of them, and was not asked about the eleven miles on any of them.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR.** Nothing changed. He was given nothing on all ten days, not offered a hook, a page, a figure or a hand, not asked to sit in the second chair, not sent on nine days, and on Chapter 426 he got up off the stones and sat down again about two feet along and nobody offered him a hand and the record about the not offering says nothing was asked and nothing was given.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES A CART, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP'S NEAR WHEEL, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY, THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-THREE WHO SELLS NOTHING AND COPIES FOR NOTHING, THE CHILD OF ABOUT EIGHT, THE BOY OF ABOUT ELEVEN WITH A HOOP, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE ON HER OWN ROUNDS, AND THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS THE TWO BOOKS.** Nothing moved. She is four hundred yards up that bank with her door shut, nobody went up that bank on any of these ten days, and the one line in the second of those two books is still in her own hand and still unread aloud.

### AND WHAT DID NOT CHANGE ABOUT ANYBODY, SAID ONCE AND MEANT

**NO AGE, NO TRADE, NO HAND, NO HOUSEHOLD, NO POSSESSION, NO STANDING, NO POST, NO APPOINTMENT, NO COLUMN, NO NAME PRINTED, NO PANEL, NO NEW PERSON, NO NEW PLACE, NO THIRD LINE, NO BELL PULLED, NO NIGHT NAMED BUT ONE, AND THE READING OF THAT LOT STILL AT THE FOURTH OF THE FIVE.** The review repaired eight sentences in three chapters and added one paragraph that is about a clerk refusing to become an instrument, **and not one of the nine changes gave a person anything, took a person anything, or made a person into a job.**

---

## VOLUME 09 BATCH 0005 — CHARACTER STATE AFTER CHAPTERS 431 TO 440, THE LAST BLOCK OF THE VOLUME

**NO AGE IS ALTERED. NO TRADE IS ALTERED. NO PERSON IS GIVEN A NAME. NO PERSON IS GIVEN A JOB, A POST, A PAGE, A FIGURE, A CHAIR OR AN INSTRUMENT ON ANY OF THESE TEN DAYS, AND THE FIGURES BELOW WERE CHECKED AGAINST THE CHAPTER FILES ONE FILE AT A TIME AFTER THE LAST PROSE EDIT.**

### THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST
**His right hand has not closed properly since the tenth of the eighth month and it is in his coat on all ten days of this block. HE WAS NOT NAMED, NOT ASKED FOR, AND NOT CALLED ANYTHING BY ANYBODY IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS.** On the third of the twelfth he said out loud, in that yard, that nothing that happened that week could have happened with his name on it, and that he has gone over it every morning for eleven days and there is no version of the last ten days where a page with his name at the top of it is the version anybody wanted, and that that is not a complaint and he is not asking anybody for anything and nobody has asked him for a name since the nineteenth of the tenth month and nobody is going to. A clerk entered that the thing contained no name and no request. **On the sixth of the twelfth he told the yard in advance that tomorrow there is going to be a hand on that book and that he was not going to say why and was not going to stop it, and a clerk entered that what he said carried no date.** He stood nine feet off that table on seven of the ten days and said nothing at all on four of them.

### THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS THE TWO BOOKS
**UNCHANGED. FOUR HUNDRED YARDS UP THAT BANK, A ROOM ABOUT NINE FEET BY ELEVEN, A SHELF, TWO BOOKS, A SHUT DOOR, A STEP OF TWO STONES, A FANLIGHT OVER THE DOOR THAT LIGHTS NOTHING. NOBODY WENT UP THAT BANK ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS OF THIS BLOCK AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD WENT UP IT. THE ONE LINE IN THE SECOND OF THOSE TWO BOOKS IS STILL IN HER OWN HAND, NOBODY ASKED HER, AND THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED. IT MAY NOT BE WRITTEN IN AGAIN, CORRECTED, STRUCK OR READ ALOUD BY ANYBODY.**

### THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS
**THE ONLY PERSON WHO CHANGED IN ANYTHING BUT A FIGURE. She wrote a date into a lot book in the open on the second of the twelfth and a clerk's entry says that is not a seventh of anything. She moved the count of instruments from ten to eleven on the third with a reason given out loud by somebody else and entered under it. She said out loud four times in ten days that a clerk who says no four times has a habit, and entered that a clerk is not going to say which of two positions she is in. SHE HEARD A NAME ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF THE ELEVENTH, WROTE ONE LINE, PUT NOTHING UNDER IT, AND AT Chapter 439 SAID OUT LOUD TO THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN AND NOT TO THE TABLE THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO PUT HER UNCHECKED MARGIN AND HIS UNWRITTEN NAME TOGETHER IN A PAGE WHILE EITHER OF THEM IS STANDING THERE. SHE WAS NOT ASKED FOR THE NAME, SHE DID NOT WRITE IT, SHE IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT AND NOT A SECOND READER AND NOT A POST, AND THE QUESTION OF WHETHER HER HEARING IS GOING WAS NOT ANSWERED.**

### THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING
**HIS LEFT HAND HAS NOT CLOSED SINCE THE ELEVENTH OF THE JUNE. THE CLOTH CAME OFF IT ON THE FIFTH OF THE TWELFTH AT ABOUT ELEVEN AND IT IS NOW IN HIS INSIDE POCKET, AND ON THE SEVENTH THAT HAND LAY FLAT AND OPEN ON THE SECOND LINE OF THE LOT BOOK FOR ABOUT FOUR MINUTES AND DID NOT CLOSE, AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAID ONE WORD ABOUT WHAT IT WAS DOING THERE. NOBODY ASKED HIM WHY, NOBODY SAID IT WAS A COMING-BACK, NOBODY THANKED HIM, AND A CLERK MAY NOT ENTER THAT IT IS BETTER THAN IT WAS AND DID NOT ENTER IT, AND DID NOT ENTER THAT IT WAS WORSE EITHER. THE STRINGS *COMING BACK*, *RECOVERED*, *A RECOVERY*, *A REDEMPTION* AND *IS CURED* ARE ALL AT ZERO ACROSS THE TEN CHAPTERS. He is not a reader and not a keeper and not a second of anything and nobody put a word over him on a page. He was asked whether he was going to be all right and refused the question with the reason that it was not the question he had said he would refuse.**

### THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM
**HIS RIGHT ARM DID NOT GO ABOVE THE LEVEL OF THAT SHOULDER ON ANY DAY OF THIS BLOCK, AND ON SEVEN OF THE TEN HE CAME UP OUT OF THE BANK TO GET THE FOUR FIGURES AT THE END OF THAT TABLE. On the fourth of the twelfth he said out loud, in four words, that a line in a book in the open is his, and then said that he has not slept a whole night about it since the second week of the ninth, and that he could have corrected it and has not, and that a wrong count is not struck by writing a correct one beside it, and went back down the bank into the water. He is the man whose mouth the figure on the second line came out of. ON THE FIFTH OF THE TWELFTH HE STOOD AT THE END OF THAT TABLE FOR ABOUT A MINUTE AND LOOKED AT THE GROUND AND DID NOT LOOK AT THE BOOK.**

### THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD
**UNCHANGED. HE CAME UP THAT LANE ON EIGHT OF THE TEN DAYS, GOT THE FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL AS HE WENT PAST, AND WAS NOT ASKED ABOUT THE ELEVEN MILES ON ANY OF THEM. At Chapter 436 a man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart said a thing out loud about a dragging wheel and a clerk entered that a figure about a man and a wheel is not a figure about a road, and that the man who keeps a road was not asked about it.**

### THE MAN OF ABOUT NINETEEN WHO COUNTS
**HE IS NOBODY'S CLERK AND HE IS NOT THE SECOND READER. HE SAID ON THE EIGHTEENTH OF THE ELEVENTH THAT HE WOULD NOT BE CHECKED ON ANYTHING FOR A FORTNIGHT, AND THAT FORTNIGHT ENDED ON THE FIRST OF THE TWELFTH, AND ON THAT MORNING HE TOLD THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX SO OUT LOUD, AND THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX SAID HE WAS SORRY ABOUT IT. HE ASKED ON THE SECOND OF THE TWELFTH WHETHER HE MAY READ THE NEW LINE BACK FROM THE TOP AND WAS TOLD NO IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS BEFORE THE CLERK ANSWERED, AND HE SAID AFTERWARDS THAT HE HAD NOT EXPECTED A QUESTION TO BE ABLE TO BE TAKEN OFF A PERSON. HE ASKED ON THE THIRD WHETHER ELEVEN OF WHAT, AND WAS TOLD, AND WAS TOLD THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO LIST THE OTHER TEN. HE PUT HIS OWN PAGE FACE DOWN ON NONE OF THESE TEN DAYS, AND THE FIFTH TIME IS NOT IN THIS BLOCK.**

### THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT
**SHE READ THE TWO LINES IN THAT LOT BOOK STANDING UP ON HER WAY PAST ON ALL TEN DAYS, AND THE THREE LINES FROM THE SECOND OF THE TWELFTH ONWARD, AND SAID NOTHING TO ANYBODY AND WAS SAID NOTHING TO BY ANYBODY, ON ALL TEN. On the thirtieth of the eleventh and on the first of the twelfth she said out loud, in a yard of about nineteen people, that she has carried water to eleven houses for nineteen years, has asked about a bucket and nothing else, has read those two lines every morning for two months, is not going to read them out loud to anybody this month, and that if somebody asks her she will say no, and that this is not a rule and is not for any of them. On the fifth she said that she has a hand that has never been what it was on the day she started and has never once said that out loud and is not saying it now to anybody in particular. SHE WAS NOT GIVEN A POST, WAS NOT ASKED TO READ ANYTHING BACK, AND HAS NO NAME ON ANYTHING.**

### THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX
**HE READ THE FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL ON EIGHT OF THE TEN DAYS AND DID NOT READ THEM ON TWO. On the thirty-first of the eleventh he spent a morning counting marks off that board instead of reading numbers, read all thirty-one of them twice out loud, and read the four figures for the first time that day at about half past five. ON THE FIRST OF THE TWELFTH HE DID NOT READ THEM AT ALL, WALKED DOWN THAT LANE AND STOOD IN FRONT OF A POST WITH A NAIL IN IT FOR ABOUT AS LONG AS IT TAKES TO FILL ONE BUCKET, CAME BACK, READ THEM TWO HOURS LATE, AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE HAS LOST ABOUT TWO HOURS OF A MORNING HE HAS BEEN DOING FOR SIX MONTHS AND THAT THE FIGURE IS NOT GOING TO MOVE BECAUSE HE WALKED DOWN A LANE. He has never been told anything about the two walls and was told nothing on these ten days. HE SAID HE WAS SORRY ABOUT A FORTNIGHT, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, TO A BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN.**

### THE ONLY MAN WHO HAS READ A THING BACK FROM THE TOP
**HE SAID ONE THING OUT LOUD IN THAT YARD ON THE THIRD OF THE TWELFTH, FOR THE FIRST TIME IN ABOUT ELEVEN VISITS, AND IT WAS ABOUT A DRAGGING WHEEL AND AN ARM THAT CANNOT LIFT IT HIGHER, AND HE WENT ON UP THE LANE AFTERWARDS. HE WAS NOT TOLD HE IS A SECOND READER, HE WAS NOT APPOINTED, NO NAME OF HIS WENT INTO ANY COLUMN, HE WAS NOT PAID, AND HE IS NOT A KEEPER OF A READING. ON THE SIXTH HE SAID THAT A MAN CAN TELL YOU A THING AND GO AWAY AND HAVE IT STAY TOLD.**

### THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP
**HE SAID A NAME OUT LOUD IN THAT YARD ON THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF THE ELEVENTH AND IT IS ON NO PAGE. IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS HE TURNED HIS TWO HANDS OVER AT THE END OF THAT TABLE AND ASKED WHAT A MAN IS SUPPOSED TO DO WITH HIS HANDS WHEN THERE IS NOTHING COMING, AND NOBODY TOLD HIM. HE GAVE THE REASON OUT LOUD AT Chapter 436 FOR A NUMBER MOVING, IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS. HIS TENT IS STILL ROLLED ON THE BACK OF THAT HAND-CART AND HAS NOT GONE UP ON ANY DAY SINCE THE THIRTIETH OF THE JUNE, HIS HANDS WERE NOT TOUCHED AND NOTHING WAS ASKED ABOUT THEM, AND HE WAS GIVEN NOTHING. HIS NAME IS NOT ON ANY PAGE AND NOBODY IN THIS BLOCK SAID IT AGAIN AND NOBODY WROTE IT DOWN.**

### THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO CUTS REEDS
**HE IS THE ONLY PERSON WHO KNOWS WHEN THE FIGURE ON THE SECOND LINE OF THAT BOOK STOPPED BEING TRUE, AND ON THE SECOND OF THE TWELFTH HE SAID THAT DAY OUT LOUD TO A YARD THAT HAD NOT ASKED HIM, IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND A CLERK WROTE IT INTO A BOOK IN THE OPEN. HE WAS NOT PAID, NOT THANKED, NOT APPOINTED TO GIVE IT TO ANYBODY, AND ON THE OTHER EIGHT DAYS THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED AND NOBODY ASKED HIM WHAT THE WATER WAS. HE STOOD ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AT THE END OF THAT TABLE ON SEVEN of the ten days and went up the lane, and nobody said one word to him on any of them.**

### THE MAN WITH THE TALLY, THE WOMAN WITH THE SCALE, THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-THREE, THE CHILD, THE BOY OF ABOUT ELEVEN, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE, THE MAN WITH THE PORTFOLIO, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-FOUR, THE BOY OF NINE, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY AT A COUNTER
**NOTHING MOVED. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY WAS ASKED FOR THE FIGURE ON NONE OF THE TEN DAYS AND A CLERK ENTERED NOTHING ABOUT HIM. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE CAME DOWN THAT BANK ON THREE OF THE TEN DAYS AND ASKED FOR NOTHING TO BE WRITTEN DOWN, AND HER FOUR EMPTY HOUSES ARE NOT OFFERED AND NOT COUNTED.**

### THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR
**A HUNDRED AND THIRTY DAYS IN RISING TO A HUNDRED AND THIRTY-NINE, AND A HUNDRED AND TWENTY-NINE OF THAT MANY NIGHTS SLEPT RISING TO A HUNDRED AND THIRTY-EIGHT. HE WAS GIVEN NOTHING ON ALL TEN DAYS. HE WAS NOT OFFERED A PAGE, A FIGURE, A CHAIR, A HAND OR A CLOTH, AND WAS NOT ASKED TO SIT IN THE SECOND CHAIR AGAIN, AND WAS NOT SENT ON NINE DAYS AND DID NOT GO, AND WAS NOT ASKED TO GO DOWN THAT LANE WITH THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX OR WITH ANYBODY ELSE. THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT OFFERING SAYS NOTHING WAS ASKED AND NOTHING WAS GIVEN ON ALL TEN. A CLERK ENTERED AT Chapter 440 THAT HE IS ON HIS HUNDRED AND THIRTY-NINTH NIGHT AND HAS SLEPT ON A HUNDRED AND THIRTY-EIGHT OF THEM, AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHAT HE IS GOING TO DO WITH HIS HANDS TONIGHT BECAUSE NOBODY ASKED HER. A CLERK DID NOT SAY THAT A MAN WHOSE TRADE IS OVER IS A FAILURE AND DID NOT SAY THAT HE IS NOT ONE EITHER.**

### AND WHAT DID NOT CHANGE ABOUT ANYBODY, SAID ONCE AND MEANT

**NO AGE, NO TRADE, NO HAND, NO HOUSEHOLD, NO POSSESSION, NO STANDING, NO POST, NO APPOINTMENT, NO COLUMN, NO NAME PRINTED, NO PANEL, NO NEW PERSON, NO NEW PLACE, NO BELL PULLED, NO NIGHT NAMED, AND THE READING OF THAT LOT STILL AT THE FOURTH OF THE FIVE. THE TEN CHAPTERS OF THIS BLOCK GAVE NO PERSON ANYTHING, TOOK NO PERSON ANYTHING, AND MADE NO PERSON INTO A JOB, AND THE CLOTH CAME OFF A HAND AND THE HAND DID NOT CLOSE AND NOBODY WAS TOLD IT WAS BETTER.**

---

## VOLUME 09 BATCH 0005 — WHAT THE REVIEW OF CHAPTERS 431 TO 440 DID TO THESE PEOPLE, AND WHAT IT DID NOT

**THE REVIEW AMENDED SEVEN SENTENCES IN SIX CHAPTERS AND NOT ONE OF THEM CHANGED AN AGE, A TRADE, A HAND, A HOUSEHOLD, A POSSESSION, A STANDING, A POST, AN APPOINTMENT, A COLUMN, A NAME PRINTED OR A PERSON. EVERY PERSON IN THIS BLOCK IS EXACTLY AS THE ENTRIES ABOVE SET HER, HIM OR THEM OUT, WITH THE SIX EXCEPTIONS BELOW, AND THE EXCEPTIONS ARE WORDS AND NOT FACTS.**

**1. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIES LOAM IS STILL THE MAN WITH THAT RIGHT ARM BELOW THE LEVEL OF THAT SHOULDER AT EVERY HOUR OF EVERY DAY, AND NOBODY SAYS ONE WORD ABOUT IT.** His paragraph at Chapter 440 lost the clause that repeated the man of fifty-six reading the four figures for the last time, and it now ends on *nobody has ever written down what time he comes out*. **HIS ARM, HIS TRADE, HIS SILENCE AND THE FACT THAT NO CHAPTER EXPLAINS IT ARE UNCHANGED, AND THE REPAIR MADE HIS LAST PARAGRAPH SHORTER AND DID NOT MAKE IT SAY ANYTHING NEW.**

**2. THE BOY OF ABOUT NINETEEN STILL IS NOT A CLERK, NOT A SECOND READER AND NOT A KEEPER OF ANYTHING, AND HIS FORTNIGHT STILL ENDS ON THE FIRST OF THE TWELFTH.** **HIS OWN MOUTH WAS ONE DAY OUT IN THE REPAIR: at Chapter 434 he said he had made the statement on *the eighteenth* of the month, and canon fixes it in his own mouth at Chapter 425 as *the seventeenth*, and he now says the seventeenth.** The repair did not change what he wants, which is still to be left alone and not counted, and did not change the fact that nobody asked him to be a second reader on any of the ten days. **HIS COUNTED CLAIMS, THE FIGURES HE GETS FOR THE WORDS IN A SPEECH, ARE UNCHANGED AND ALL ELEVEN OF THE BLOCK'S ELEVEN CLAIMS STILL REPRODUCE, ON THE UNCHANGED PER-CHAPTER COLUMN OF 1, 1, 1, 2, 2, 1, 2, 0, 0, 1.**

**3. THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX STILL READS FOUR FIGURES OFF THAT WALL EVERY MORNING AND GETS ALL FOUR, AND ON Chapter 433 HE STILL SPENT THE MORNING COUNTING A MONTH INSTEAD AND READ THEM FOR THE FIRST TIME THAT DAY AT ABOUT HALF PAST FIVE.** **THE REPAIR REBUILT THAT MORNING'S CLOCK AND CHANGED NOTHING ABOUT HIM: one rate of about four minutes a mark, a first pass from about ten past eight to about twenty past ten, a second pass to about half past twelve, and the cost of the day is the same cost, which is that he was watched standing still and nobody asked him why.**

**4. THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS IS STILL THE VOLUME'S INSTRUMENT AND NOT A CLERK OF A HOUSE, STILL ENTERING WHAT SHE SAW AND NOT WHAT SHE THOUGHT, AND STILL NOT AN INSTRUMENT, NOT A POST AND NOT A SECOND READER.** **THE WORD *UNCHECKED* IS STILL IN HER OWN MARGIN OVER NOTHING, AND IT IS STILL THERE AT Chapter 440 BECAUSE SHE SAYS OUT LOUD THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO TAKE IT OFF, AND SHE STILL IS NOT GOING TO.** **THE COUNT IS TEN ACROSS THESE TEN DAYS, ONE A CHAPTER, AND THE REVIEW CORRECTED *ELEVEN* TO *TEN* IN THE THREE DOCUMENTS THAT SAID ELEVEN; THE WORD HERSELF HAS NOT BEEN TOUCHED AND WAS NOT TOUCHED IN THE PROSE.** The one corrupted word in her Chapter 438 entry, *tableble*, is now *table*, and the entry's content is unchanged.

**5. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING STILL HAS HIS LEFT HAND IN ITS CLOTH AND HIS RIGHT HAND FLAT ON THE BOARDS, AND AT Chapter 440 THE HAND ON THE SECOND LINE OF THAT LOT BOOK IS HIS.** The repair did not say whose hand it is, did not make it close, and did not let a clerk enter that it was better or worse. **Chapter 438's cloth still comes off and the hand still does not close, and the corrupted word in that day's closing entry is repaired and the entry still says what it said.**

**6. THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST IS STILL UNNAMED, STILL UNASKED, AND NOBODY IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS CALLS HIM ANYTHING AT ALL.** The repair moved only the hour at which he comes down that bank to watch, from *about one* to *about twenty past twelve*, so that the eleven minutes he watches are the last eleven minutes of a count that has already been given a clock. **HE STILL SAYS NOTHING WHILE HE WATCHES IT, AND HE STILL SAYS OUT LOUD THAT HE WORKED OUT WHICH OF THE TWO HE IS ON THE THIRTIETH OF THE TENTH AND WAS NOT ASKED TO SAY SO THEN EITHER.**

**AND WHAT DID NOT CHANGE ABOUT ANYBODY AT ALL.** The man of about thirty-seven who cuts reeds is still the only person who knows the day the figure on the second line stopped being true, and he was still not paid, not thanked and not appointed to give anything to anybody, and he still gave the day out loud to a yard that had not asked him. The man of about sixty-four is still at the foot of that low wall with his coat folded on the stones and nothing in his hands, given nothing, not offered a chair, not sent on nine days. The woman of fifty-eight still read the two lines and then the three lines standing up and said nothing to anybody. The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale still came down that bank and said the one thing she said and it is still the only figure anybody gave out loud about that ditch that anybody wrote down. The man the figure of twenty-one years is against is not a second reader and was not made one. **NO NAME, AGE, TRADE, HAND, HOUSEHOLD, POSSESSION, STANDING, POST, APPOINTMENT, COLUMN, PANEL, NEW PERSON OR NEW PLACE EXISTS IN THIS BLOCK THAT DID NOT EXIST BEFORE THE REPAIR.**
